# Wayvida Books — Transfer to Another AI

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Day Book accounting projection)

Day Book remains a report over shared posted journals, not a second ledger. `src/daybook-service.js` now records the source party, account codes and audit metadata on its read projection; account filters select whole vouchers so total debit and credit cannot be changed by hiding counterpart lines. A cancelled source document is absent from the normal report and appears only with `includeCancelled`; drafts remain in the explicitly non-accounting review view. `src/DayBook.jsx` supplies the report drill-down and read-only export/print actions. Continue from `docs/DAY-BOOK.md`; do not add report-side transaction editing or inferred source/audit data.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Create Vendor: type dropdown, four fields a line, Status off the form)

Three changes to the Create Vendor page on the shared `form.itemCreatePage` shell, all in `src/Vendors.jsx` and `src/vendors.css`. The vendor type stopped being the `fieldset.itemTypeField` radio group that led the form and became one **dropdown inside the field grid** - `field('type','Vendor type *',{options:types})`, the same `select` renderer every other field uses, still writing the stored `type` key - so the `itemTypeField` / `itemRadioGroup` rules are gone from the vendor stylesheet (they stay in `src/items.css` for the Item and Customer forms). The grid went from **three fields a line to four** (`.vendorsPage .itemCreatePage .itemFormGrid{grid-template-columns:repeat(4,minmax(0,1fr))}`, still two below 1100px and one below 700px), which leaves Vendor details as two tidy rows: type, name, code, display name / contact person, phone, email, website. And **Status left the create page entirely**. That is a UI decision, not a data one: a new vendor still takes `status:'Active'` from `vendorDefaults`, `save()` still spreads `{...form}` so an edit keeps whatever the record already had, and the register row menu plus the detail screen are still the only places the status changes. `tests/vendor-ui.test.mjs` now pins all of it, including `status:'Active'` in `src/vendor-store.js`, so the default the removed field used to carry cannot quietly disappear. Measured live at 1280px: four 219.3px columns over eight labels, the first control a `SELECT`, no `Status` label on the page, no page overflow. Evidence: `tests/vendor-ui.test.mjs` 11/11, the vendor store suite green, the full sweep at **813 tests / 765 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures** (unchanged), a clean build (`index-CfgL4Dky.js` 1,811.23 kB), and `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs`.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Note: Tax summary and Accounting Impact Preview removed, place of supply into the Adjustment row)

The operator asked for two things on the create page and both are done. The **Tax summary** card and the collapsed **Accounting Impact Preview** are **gone**, which reverses that part of the earlier same-day brief (its §9 and §10) - the newest instruction wins, and the QA record says so. The page now runs Customer, Credit note details, Original invoice summary, Credit items or Adjustment, Credit summary, Additional details. The removal was done properly rather than by leaving dead weight: `impactOpen`, the `creditPostingPlan(db,{...form,totals},totals)` call, the `creditPostingPlan` import and all `.creditNotes .cnImpactToggle*` rules went too, and the Credit summary hint was reworded because it had been pointing at the sections that no longer exist. Nothing was lost: the Credit summary still closes on its `['Total tax',t.cgst+t.sgst+t.igst+t.cess]` line, and the account-mapping refusal still fires from `creditPostingPlan` inside `issue()`, so an incomplete mapping is reported at Post and on the detail page's Accounting tab. The second ask was **place of supply in the Adjustment card, four to a line**: `soFieldRow` became `soFieldRow4`, the field is read-only and inherited from the invoice on the amount-based against-invoice path (`32 · Kerala`) and stays a select on a standalone value-only note, and the standalone *Original invoice details* card - which held nothing else - is hidden while the Adjustment card shows. Measured live: four 222.8px columns at 1280px with every label at the same top, and no page-level overflow at 1280 or 420. Tests: `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` 23/23, `tests/credit-note.test.mjs` 17/17, full sweep **813 / 765 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures** (unchanged from before this pass), clean build `index-0lSk4lM8.js` 1,811.58 kB. When a section leaves a page here, take its state, its CSS, its imports and its now-stale copy with it - and repoint the test that asserted it rather than deleting the test.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Note: credit method, amount-based credit, inventory impact, posting scope)

The Credit Note create page grew the fields and the flow the latest brief asked for, on top of the existing components, engine and lifecycle. **Credit method** is a new first-class field (`CREDIT_METHODS` = `Item Based Credit` / `Amount Based Credit`, normalised by `creditMethod()`, stored on the note). An amount-based note has no quantities at all: `calculateCredit` builds ONE synthetic line with `invoiceItemIndex:-1`, tax comes from `billedTaxRates(invoice)` - the invoice's own blended component rates, exact for a single-rate invoice - or from `chosenTaxRates(form,taxPolicy)` on a standalone note (the operator's `GST 18%`-style Tax treatment, split into CGST+SGST inside the company state and IGST outside it), and the caps are enforced in total per tax component instead of per invoice line. Its card holds Adjustment amount / Tax treatment / Adjustment account, the last of which is the one documented exception to "mappings, not document choices": `creditPostingPlan` reads `note.adjustmentAccount` ahead of `mapping[role]`, so the form offers Income accounts only (the type the plan validates the revenue line against). **Inventory impact** (`INVENTORY_IMPACTS` = `Return Stock To Inventory` / `Financial Adjustment Only`) is now an explicit field on an item-based note and is what sets `salesReturn.enabled`, so returning goods is the operator's stated choice rather than something inferred from the reason; the old `cnReturnToggle` checkbox is gone. The note also stores `companyId` / `companyName` / `branchId` / `branchName` read from `getCurrentOrganizationContext()`, shown as a read-only strip on the details card, and `calculateCredit` refuses when a note's company disagrees with its invoice's. Smaller additions: the head carries a `soHeadStatus` + `StatusPill` badge reading the note's own `creditNoteStatus`; the customer strip states **Available credit** beside Net receivable (the shared `.soCustomerMeta` item becomes a two-column grid on this page); the Original invoice details card leads with a seven-fact `dl.cnInvoiceSummary` (number, date, total, already credited, available credit, GST treatment, place of supply) above the link that still opens the full snapshot in the right-side popup; the tax summary and the credit summary each close on a **Total tax** line; and the posting preview is a collapsed **Accounting Impact Preview** - a React `button.cnImpactToggle` with `aria-expanded` and `{impactOpen&&<>...`, not a `details` - that now renders for standalone notes too, because the tax summary and the preview moved out of the `{original&&<>...}` fragment into `{totals&&<>...}`. `Without Invoice` became **Standalone Credit Note** in `CREDIT_NOTE_TYPES`, with `LEGACY_NOTE_TYPES` in `noteType()` keeping the old label readable - a naive rename would have re-read every stored standalone note as an against-invoice one. Evidence: `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` 23/23, `tests/credit-note.test.mjs` 17/17, the full sweep at **813 tests / 765 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures** (baseline 806 / 758 / 48), a clean build (`index-CZTF7Urw.js` 1,814.22 kB), `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs`, and a live pass at 1280px covering both methods, both inventory decisions, the seven-fact summary, the Total tax lines and a balanced ₹590.00 value-only posting preview, with no page-level horizontal overflow at 1280 or 420.

**Two failures this pass taught something.** Neither the production build nor the server-rendered test suite can see a defect that only exists once the create form actually mounts: `start()` referenced `DEFAULT_CREDIT_METHOD` and `DEFAULT_INVENTORY_IMPACT` that were missing from the `./credit-note-service.js` import, so opening New credit note threw `Uncaught ReferenceError: DEFAULT_CREDIT_METHOD is not defined` while the build stayed green and every test passed; and a fragment conversion left one extra `}` that rendered as a literal brace on the page. `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` now asserts the exact import clause and the clean `</div></div>{totals&&(()=>{` boundary. Open Credit Notes -> New credit note in the running app after touching this page - the suites render the register, not the form. Known limits left open: the amount-based adjustment has no Cess field of its own, its standalone Tax treatment is a fixed rate list rather than the per-line `TaxSelection` control, and organisation/branch are read from the working context rather than chosen on the document.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Note original-invoice facts as a right-side popup)

The **Original invoice details** card on the Credit Note create page is one link plus a popup now. `button.cnInvoiceLink` (rendering `Invoice INV-00001 →` inside `span.cnInvoiceFacts`) opens the invoice snapshot in a right-side drawer instead of unfolding a full fact grid inside the card, which is what the operator asked for - "Fix that if we click that invoice id show that details in a right popup", following "### Original invoice details show as a link with invoice id... No need of these text just invoice link and text if click just come popup with all the invoice data". The drawer is `createPortal(..., window.document.body)` into `div.cnInvoiceLayer` (`position:fixed;inset:0;z-index:120;display:flex;justify-content:flex-end`) holding `button.cnInvoiceBackdrop`, and `div.cnInvoiceDrawer` (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-label="Original invoice details"`, `position:relative`, `width:min(430px,94vw)`, `height:100%`) whose head prints `Original invoice details` over the invoice number and carries `button.cnInvoiceDrawerClose` (`IconX`), then one `div.cnInvoiceDrawerBody` scroller. All thirteen facts are unchanged and still read-only. The open state is `invoiceFactsId`, set to `form.originalInvoiceId`, so changing the invoice or leaving the form closes the drawer with no effect needed; Escape (a `keydown` listener live only while it is open), the backdrop and the close button each clear it through `setInvoiceFactsId(null)`. The old `.cnInvoiceFacts > summary` / `[open]` rules left `src/credit-notes.css` with them. **Trap worth remembering:** the first cut used `<aside className="cnInvoiceDrawer">` and the drawer measured `transform:matrix(1,0,0,1,-404.8,0)` at 420px - a full width off the left edge, invisible, while every other measurement read as correct. The cause is the legacy global rule `@media(max-width:700px){aside{transform:translateX(-100%);transition:.2s;width:250px}}` in `src/styles.css`, which exists to hide the app sidebar on a phone and catches every `aside` on the page. The drawer is a `div` now and its own CSS carries no `transform`; never put a portalled popup back on an `aside`. Evidence: `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` **19/19** with one test added (the link is a `button` and not a `details`, the portal mounts on `document.body`, the dialog is named, no `aside`, backdrop/close/Escape each dismiss, the layer is fixed and right-aligned, the drawer is `min(430px,94vw)` folding to `100%` under 560px, one scrolling body, one-column facts); `tests/credit-note.test.mjs` 14/14; the full sweep is **806 tests / 758 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures** (baseline 805 / 757 / 48, so one test added and nothing broken); a clean production build (`index-C77Yjbch.js` 1,807.17 kB); and a live pass on the running preview at 1280px and at 420px showing the drawer flush to the right edge (430px and 405px wide), `transform:none`, `visible:true` and `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at both widths, with all thirteen facts present.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (ad-hoc invoice lines, the receipt role panel, the broken receipt row menu)

Three defects fixed. (1) An ad-hoc item could not be invoiced: blankDocumentLine() in src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx started every line - and so every ad-hoc line, since onAdHoc resets through it - with priceTaxMode:'', and the engine refuses a line whose price type is neither exclusive nor inclusive, so saving threw Choose whether each line rate includes tax. Reproduced against the engine directly: '' fails, 'exclusive' saves. The blank line now starts tax exclusive (the Indian convention, and what every Item master record and itemDefaults already use), so an ad hoc item can be added and the invoice saved, and the line own select still switches it to inclusive. (2) The Receipt role local-simulation panel is hidden: the receiptRole block and its paragraph are gone from src/Receipts.jsx with the now-unused RECEIPT_ROLES import, while permissions still run through receiptAllowed with the page default role. (3) The receipt detail 3-dot menu no longer breaks the page: it rendered in normal flow because .receiptWorkspace .receiptMore>div was forced position:static with a 230px last column, which pushed the detail header, tabs and stat cells around when opened. It is now the canonical portalled menu (soMoreButton + createPortal + soActionMenu from src/sales-order-actions.css, closed on outside pointer, Escape or a choice) and the six rules that forced it into flow were deleted. Evidence: 805 tests / 757 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures; tests/item-tax-ui.test.mjs repointed from priceTaxMode:'' to 'exclusive'; tests/receipt-ui.test.mjs repointed to assert the role panel is absent and to assert the portal instead of the old static-menu CSS; a clean build (index-DYBj1LTr.js 1,798.80 kB); and a live pass showing no role panel, the menu rendered on document.body with all seven entries, the page intact and no horizontal overflow. Still open: restyling the NEW RECEIPT page onto the Create Sales Invoice shell, which this pass deliberately did not half-apply.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Notes register: nine columns and a four-entry row menu)

The register now states the nine columns the operator asked for: **Date, Credit note id, Reference, Customer name, Invoice, Status, Amount, Balance, Actions**. The earlier Credit note type, Reason, Applied amount, Remaining credit and Created By left the grid - the type and reason remain register filters and read on the detail page, and the applied figure stays on the Applications tab. The number is the link into the detail, the invoice column prints an em dash with no invoice, Amount is the credit total and Balance is the remaining credit. The row action is one canonical 3-dot menu with exactly **Edit, Preview, Duplicate, Delete**: Edit opens the stored note in the create form and is disabled unless it is a draft (the engine edits the latest draft only), Preview opens the print preview, Duplicate (`duplicateCredit`) opens a fresh draft copied from the note, and Delete runs the module's cancellation - reason required, posted journal and applications reversed, audit trail kept, because a posted credit note is never erased. Lifecycle steps (submit, approve, issue) and allocation/refund stay on the detail page and the Applications tab, so nothing is lost by dropping them from the row. `src/invoice-workspace.css` restates the nine widths to total 100%, right-aligns Amount and Balance, and moves the status refund line from column 10 to 6. Evidence: 803 tests / 755 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures, `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` 17/17 (the register test replaced, plus a new row-menu test proving the four entries and the absence of the lifecycle labels), a clean build (`index-B2lMTu6_.js` 1,799.07 kB), and a live register rendering the nine headers with no overflow. The row menu was not opened live because the stored register has no credit notes; the test covers it.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Note account mapping: both routes offered)

The mapping dialog now exposes both routes rather than one. The shared **Sales Adjustment Account** remains the account every reason uses by default, and beside it sit the three reason-specific routes the engine already resolved but nothing could set: **Sales Return Account** (Sales Return), **Tax Adjustment Account** (GST Correction) and **Other Adjustment Account**. Each is blank by default and its placeholder reads `Use the Sales Adjustment account`, so blank means inherit and a set value pins that reason - which is how a dedicated sales-return ledger is kept while every other reason follows the shared account. Only **Sales Adjustment** and **Accounts Receivable** are required now; `Inventory Asset` and `Stock Adjustment` became optional, because they matter only when a goods return moves stock and `raiseSalesReturn()` refuses then - the previous `required` blanks stopped the dialog saving at all. `creditMappingDefaults()` returns the eight effective values. Evidence: 802 tests / 754 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures, `tests/credit-note.test.mjs` 13/13 (the new test proves a `salesReturn` account wins for its reason while `salesAdjustment` covers the rest, both in the plan and in the posted journal), `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` 16/16 (the new test pins both routes, the two required accounts and the inherit wording), a clean build (`index-CjAeQYvn.js` 1,799.70 kB), and a live dialog showing all eight fields with the three inherit placeholders.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Note account mapping now resolves instead of refusing)

The reported blocker was real and is fixed: `creditPostingPlan()` resolved the revenue/adjustment account only from `mapping[role] || mapping.salesAdjustment`, so a fresh install (no `config.creditMappings`) refused every posting with "Credit Note account mapping is incomplete". The chain is now the role account, then the shared `salesAdjustment`, then the income account of the adjusted invoice line, then `s.config.sales` - so an invoice-backed note reverses the revenue that invoice recognised, and a dedicated sales-return ledger remains available by configuring `salesReturn`/`salesAdjustment`. GST and the receivable already resolved through equivalent fallbacks, so all three lines now behave alike, and the engine still refuses when nothing can resolve (no mapping, no Sales account, no invoice income) - the atomicity test asserts exactly that case. A new exported `creditMappingDefaults(s)` feeds the **Account mapping** dialog (register caret and the Accounting tab's Go to Account Mapping), which opens on the effective accounts rather than blank selects. Evidence: 800 tests / 752 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures, `tests/credit-note.test.mjs` 12/12 (one new test proving a mapping-free post reverses the invoice's income account and balances), `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` 15/15 after repointing the seeding assertion, a clean build (`index-FJM4MrEr.js` 1,798.91 kB), and a live preview that resolves `4000 · Sales` with no warning where it previously refused.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (Credit Note module: invoice-first flow, tax and posting previews, twelve-column register)

One request, in `src/CreditNotes.jsx`, `src/credit-note-service.js`, `src/credit-notes.css` and `src/invoice-workspace.css`: update the Credit Note module's UI and workflow to proper ERP logic without rebuilding it. The engine, storage keys, validations and accounting stay. What changed: `creditPostingPlan(s,note,totals)` in the service is the one ordered account plan (reason-driven revenue/adjustment account, one GST reversal per component, round off, then the receivable credit) and `issue` now posts through it, so the new Accounting Preview on the form cannot disagree with the journal; `creditNoteStatus` returns `Approved` for an approved unposted note instead of falling through to `Issued`, and `Approved` joined `CREDIT_NOTE_STATUSES` and the pill tones. The create page is now six sections in flow order - Credit note details (number generated and READ-ONLY, date, type, customer, reason, reason detail, reference), Original invoice details (picker plus the read-only invoice facts, including remaining available credit), Credit items, Tax summary, Accounting preview, and a collapsed Additional details holding the customer note, the internal note and the attachments. The customer is asked for before the invoice and the invoice list shows only that customer's posted invoices. The items table is Item, Description, Original quantity, Previously credited, Available quantity, Credit quantity, Unit, Rate, Discount, Tax, Amount and remove, auto-loaded from the invoice, with the credit quantity capped at the available quantity; the per-row invoice-item picker is deleted because the rows are the invoice items, and only `Add invoice item` survives for a removed row. Tax is inherited and has no control anywhere. The register states Date, Credit note number, Customer, Credit note type, Original invoice, Reason, Amount, Applied amount, Remaining credit, Status, Created By and Actions, with its twelve widths restated to total 100%. Deliberate non-changes: the stored lifecycle stays Draft/Pending Approval/Approved/Issued/Cancelled with the applied and refunded words derived, so "Posted" is the existing Issued state and no second status vocabulary was invented; amounts are never recalculated in the UI, the tax summary and the preview print engine values only. Evidence: 798 tests / 750 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures, with `tests/credit-note.test.mjs` 11/11 unchanged (the proof the posting refactor moved no accounting rule) and `tests/credit-note-ui.test.mjs` 14/14 after five repointed assertions and three new tests, a clean build (`index-FSr6piYf.css` 661.12 kB, `index-CnsJ-9Ty.js` 1,795.63 kB), `scripts/prepare-sites-build.mjs`, and a live pass in the in-app browser reading every new section, the capped quantity, the tax summary, the balanced accounting preview and its mapping-refusal sentence.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-26 (the printed sheet owns its table geometry)

One reported defect: the sales order preview broke out of the page. A preview is mounted INSIDE a register page, so the host's table rules were reaching the printed sheet - `src/items.css` declares `.itemsPage table{width:100%;border-collapse:collapse;min-width:800px;text-align:left}`, and `src/ui-system.css` pads every `th`/`td` with `16px 20px !important` plus a 24px first-child left inset and a 24px last-child right inset, with `th{white-space:nowrap!important}`. The eight-column items table therefore measured exactly 800px inside a 620px paper content box and printed 150px past the right page edge, with `HSN / SAC` and `Qty / Unit` wrapped onto two lines. The fix lives in `src/document-preview-fix.css`, whose stated job is isolating previews from legacy shell selectors: it restates `.dpPaper table{min-width:0}`, the sheet `th`/`td` padding (10px 6px / 12px 6px, 12px 6px and 14px 6px for `.dpItemsTable`), the 6px first-child left inset and the 6px last-child right inset under `!important`. `src/document-preview.css` rebalanced the eight positional widths from `4/24/10/10/12/12/12/16` to `4/21/13/12/12/12/10/16` percent (still 100) so both two-word headers print on one line. Never let a host page's table rule reach a printed sheet, never give the sheet table a `min-width`, and never add a column without restating every positional width. Evidence: `node --test "tests/*.test.mjs"` at 796 tests / 748 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures with two new passing tests in `tests/document-preview-ui.test.mjs` and one repointed assertion in `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs`, measured live in the in-app browser (paper `scrollWidth === clientWidth === 680`, table 620px ending exactly on the content box edge of 675, all eight headers single-line, uniform cell padding), and the invoice and receipt sheets measured the same way. No amount, template, storage key or posting path changed.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-25 (sales order and invoice audit fixes)

A code-level audit of the Sales Order and Invoice modules produced a findings list, and this pass implemented the control and correctness fixes it could make safely. In `src/invoice-engine.js`: `command(state, action, payload, ctx)` now enforces `invoiceAllowed(role, action)` when a role is supplied (Sales Executive and Accountant save/submit/send; Finance Manager and Admin also post/cancel/recurring), **posting requires `Pending Approval`**, the invoice must carry its own `organizationId` and `branchId` to save or post, `nextInvoiceNumber` scans for the first free number instead of counting records, and the audit entry records `by` (actor or role) plus `from`/`to` status. The role-less call keeps the engine's older contract on purpose - it is a compatibility shim for tests and importers, **not** an authentication boundary, exactly as `receiptCommand` documents its own; never let a page call `command` without a role.

`convertSalesOrder(state, order, ctx)` now refuses anything but a Confirmed order and dates the invoice when it is raised (`date = today()`, `dueDate = today + terms`) instead of inheriting the order date, because dating it back put revenue in the wrong period and a closed period would block the posting later. In the UI, cancelling an order is blocked while a live invoice exists, converting and completing require Confirmed, `trail(order, action, from)` records the acting role and the status transition, and `InvoiceWorkspace` derives the role from the View as switch, passes it to every command, renders "Approve & post" only for a Pending Approval invoice whose viewer may post, and shows `ivPermissionNote` naming who can when they may not.

`applyCustomerSnapshot` and `chooseCustomer` in `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx` now freeze `customerGstin`, `customerGstTreatment` and `customerState` on the document, the create page reports the frozen GSTIN ahead of the live master, and - because `src/DocumentPreview.jsx` already printed `GSTIN: {partyGstin}` from `doc.customerGstin` - the printed tax invoice carries the recipient GSTIN for the first time. Editing a customer can no longer rewrite the identity on an issued invoice.

`src/GeneralLedgerPro.jsx` lost its fabricated sample ledger and its hardcoded sample account list: an empty journal store renders the component's own empty state, and the Branch and Cost-centre filter options are derived from the loaded rows (real lines carry a branch **id**, so the old hardcoded names matched nothing). The Sales Orders register lost the shipment filter that nothing could satisfy, along with the dead `shipment`/`delivery`/`salesperson` form keys. Evidence: 776 tests / 728 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures, the build at 6,982 modules, and direct engine calls proving each gate. **Still open** (each needs a design decision, see AGENTS.md and `docs/TESTING-AND-QA.md`): inventory relief and COGS on a sale, separate output tax accounts, reverse charge and sales TDS/TCS, incomplete draft saves, a delivery document with reservation, returning ad-hoc lines to stock, a per-organisation GST registration, and field-level audit tracking.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-25 (searchable customer, icon fields, tax block moved)

The sales create pages took four more user requests, all in `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx`, `src/SalesOrders.jsx`, `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` and `src/sales-orders.css`. **The customer field is a plain search box** (`CustomerSearch`), not the shared `SearchSelect`, which is untouched and still serves the customer form. `+ Add item` is `details.soAddItem` (`summary.soAddItemMain`, `div.soAddItemMenu`) offering an existing item, an ad hoc line (`addLine("adHoc")` marks it `adHoc:true`, its name is typed into `input.soAdHocName`, and the engine refuses it until a price type is chosen), and Create a new item, which sets `ITEM_CREATE_KEY` from `src/quick-create.js` and navigates to Items; `src/Items.jsx` reads that key once on mount, clears it and opens its create form. Both host pages stash the draft in `DRAFT_KEY` before leaving. Additional details is four `section.soMoreGroup` blocks (Shipment, Notes & terms, History, Documents) and the line tax now reads `GST <rate>%` with the component split kept inside its menu. Evidence: 72/72 across `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` and `tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` (four new tests), a full sweep of 775 tests / 727 passed / the same 48 pre-existing failures, the build clean at 6,982 modules, and live measurement at 1440px on both create pages.


## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-25 (journal status vocabulary and cancellation)

The journal module's status values are **Draft, Pending Approval, Approved, Rejected, Reversed, Cancelled**, and they are deliberately the same words budgets, credit notes, invoices and inventory adjustments already store. Period Lock counts pending journal approvals with `status==='Pending Approval'`, so the earlier Pending/Published spelling in this module made that count read zero; do not reintroduce it. `normaliseStatus()` in `src/simple-journal-transaction.js` maps the retired spellings on read (Pending -> Pending Approval, Published and Posted -> Approved) and nothing rewrites stored records, so `TRANSACTION_STATUSES`, `JOURNAL_ACTIONS` and `POSTING_STATUS` are the single source of truth and the tests assert them.

Only Approved posts: `postsToLedger(status)` is the one rule. Cancellation is a new, unposted step reachable from Pending Approval - it requires a reason, stores `cancelledBy`/`cancelledAt`/`cancellationReason` and keeps the record for the audit trail; a draft is still deleted. Reversal now refuses a record with no `ledgerJournalId`, and the row menu hides Reverse for it. The approval action key is `Approve` and is re-validated in `action()` (duty, status, prior posting) and in the advanced editor's `commit()` through `journalAllowed(role,'post')`. The pending detail's Overview lists Status, Transaction Type, Transaction Name, Date, Pay From, Category, Amount, Organisation and Branch from `journalRegisterDisplay()`.

Evidence: 57/57 across the eight journal and demo suites, a full sweep of 767 tests / 714 passed / the same 53 pre-existing failures, the production build at 6,982 modules, and a live check of the register's status filter. Three components at the end of `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` (`BusinessJournalRegister`, `BusinessJournalForm`, `BusinessJournalDetail`) are unreferenced dead code that was updated to the new vocabulary; they can be deleted by a later pass.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-25 (journal approval gate, Journal details and header)

The journal module was reviewed against a request to simplify journal creation, remove header chrome and guarantee that only approved journals affect accounting. Most of the requested behaviour already existed and was reused rather than rebuilt: the five transaction types are already stored as the stable values `expense`, `income`, `transfer`, `customer_payment` and `vendor_payment` (`TRANSACTION_TYPES`); Pay From, Category, Amount, Organisation and Branch already come from the account master, the working context and the existing scope helpers; the lifecycle already ran Draft -> Pending -> Published with Pending -> Rejected and Published -> Reversed; and publishing already posted through `journal()` with the idempotent `journal-transaction:<id>` token. Read `src/simple-journal-transaction.js` and `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` before changing any of that.

What this checkpoint actually changed. (1) `src/simple-journal-transaction.js` now exports `POSTING_STATUS` and `postsToLedger(status)` so "only an approved journal reaches the ledger" is one testable rule: Draft, Pending, Rejected and Reversed are false and Published is the only true. (2) The single `action()` in `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` now re-checks the duty (`journalAllowed(role,duty)` from `JOURNAL_ACTION_DUTY`), the status (`JOURNAL_ACTIONS[status]` must still offer the step) and prior posting (a record holding a `ledgerJournalId` is refused) before it posts, so hiding a button is never the only protection. (3) The detail Audit Trail prints the submission, approval, rejection and posting stamps, reading `publishedBy`/`publishedAt` and still accepting the older `approvedBy`/`approvedAt`. (4) `src/SimpleTransactionForm.jsx` opens its card with one full-width **Journal details** section head and states the Pay From and Category guidance as visible helper text instead of inside the closed select. (5) The application header no longer renders a global search and the six search-specific rules left `src/styles.css`. (6) The double-encoded text in `src/App.jsx` and `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` was repaired and the byte-order mark was removed from the first line of `src/App.jsx`.

Do not reintroduce a second status vocabulary for the names in that request: draft, pending approval, approved and rejected map onto Draft, Pending, Published and Rejected, and cancellation maps onto the existing delete and reversal workflows. The evidence for this checkpoint is `node --test tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs tests/journal-detail-actions.test.mjs tests/simple-journal-transaction.test.mjs` (38/38, six tests added), the full sweep at 767 tests / 714 passed / 53 pre-existing failures with an unchanged failing set, the production build at 6,982 modules, and a live check of the header, the body text and the Record Transaction page. The approval step is labelled **Approve** in the user interface while the action key stays `Publish` and the status stays `Published`; do not rename either, because `TRANSACTION_STATUSES`, `JOURNAL_ACTIONS` and `normaliseStatus` are asserted by tests and the legacy `Posted` wording depends on the mapping.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-25 (bank ledger startup repair)

`src/invoice-engine.js` holds `RESERVED_POSTING_ACCOUNTS` - the single table of system posting ledgers - and `account()` is the only guard that accepts or repairs one. A journal line carries only an account code, so since this checkpoint the repair runs whether or not the caller names the expected type. This mattered because three callers already healed `1010 · Bank` privately (`ensureDefaultBankAccount()` in `src/purchase-service.js`, `ensure()` in `src/operations-store.js`, and `ensure()` in `src/banking-service.js`) while `journal()` refused the same ledger through its untyped validation.

The failure it fixes: `bootstrapDemoData()` in `src/demo-data.js` re-runs whenever `wayvida-demo-data-version` is below `DEMO_VERSION`, reuses a stored accounting state that already has invoices, and then posts sample transactions whose money account is the hardcoded bank ledger `1010`. If that ledger was missing or deactivated, `journal()` threw `Configure an active posting ledger account for 1010.` before the marker was written, so the throw repeated on every reload and the whole app showed **Wayvida Books could not start.** while the stored data stayed intact. The checkpoint adds `1010 · Bank` (Assets, Debit, Cash and Bank) to the reserved table, applies the repair to untyped journal lines, and makes `account()` name the account's own type when the caller named none. Nothing else changed: no storage key, posting rule, report balance, or custom-mapping validation.

Focused tests are `node tests/invoice-engine.test.mjs` (21 passed, including `a journal line repairs the reserved 1010 Bank ledger instead of stopping the load` and `an inactive account outside the reserved set is refused and names its own type`) and `node tests/demo-data.test.mjs` (6 passed, including `a stored ledger set without the bank account still finishes the demo bootstrap`). The full sweep on 2026-09-25 is 761 tests / 708 passed / 53 pre-existing failures, against a measured baseline of 705 passed / 56 failures with the source change reverted, so the change broke nothing.

## Resume checkpoint - 2026-09-24 (Journal Entry transaction lifecycle)

The Journal Entries module is transaction-first end to end. `src/SimpleTransactionForm.jsx` is the **Record Transaction** create/edit page: a Transaction type dropdown (Expense, Income, Transfer, Customer Payment, Vendor Payment), Transaction name, Date, the type's own money fields, Amount, Organisation and Branch, then a collapsed Additional details section (Description, Reference number, attachments). Type-specific fields are Pay From + Category, Deposit To + Category, From Account + To Account, Customer + optional Invoice + Deposit To, and Vendor + optional Bill + Pay From; a settlement shows the document number, date, total and open balance. The normal journey never says Debit, Credit or journal line - the double entry lives behind the collapsed **View accounting entry** block. `src/simple-journal-transaction.js` owns the vocabulary, the `journalAllowed()` duty table, validation, summary, posting lines, `nextJournalNumber()`, `duplicateTransaction()`, `reversalRecord()`, `MANUAL_JOURNAL_KEY` and the demo sample definitions; `src/record-transaction.css` owns the page.

The lifecycle is Draft -> Pending -> Published, Pending -> Rejected, Rejected -> Resubmit and Published -> Reversed. Only Draft and Rejected are editable. One `action()` in `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` performs every step, rejection stores a reason and who rejected it, publishing stores `publishedBy`/`publishedAt` and posts through `journal()` with the `Journal Transaction` source and one idempotent token per record. A settlement against an invoice or bill is posted by `invoiceCommand('pay')` or `recordPurchasePayment()`, never by a second journal. `normaliseStatus()` maps the earlier vocabulary (`Pending Approval` and `Approved` to Pending, `Posted` to Published) so stored rows keep working.

The register is nine columns - Journal ID & Date, Transaction details, Account, Transaction Type, Amount, Status, Organisation, Branch, Actions - with no Debit or Credit column, the amount taken from `journalRecordAmount()`. Search covers Journal ID, transaction name and reference; the Filters panel covers Status, Transaction type, Account, Organisation, Branch, Date range, From and To. `src/journal-register-display.js` projects name, type, account, category, party and status without mutating stored records. The acting role follows the View as switch (`reviewRole`), so Publish, Reject and Reverse are filtered out of the menu for a role that may not approve. Rows projected from another module are marked `automatic` and keep Preview only.

`src/JournalPreview.jsx` leads with a Transaction summary block and keeps the double entry under **Accounting detail**. `src/demo-data.js` (DEMO_VERSION 2) seeds the register with JE-2026-000121 through JE-2026-000127 and a REV- reversal covering every status and every type, settles a demo invoice and bill through their engines, and now seeds its own working context plus `periodOptions`, because a fresh install previously had no period to post against and the seeded registers were empty. Follow-up on the same day: the register's third column is now **Transaction details** (the name stacked over its reference), the entry mode is one **Simple / Advanced** `ModeToggle` shared by both editors, the **Wayvida will record** summary is opt-in behind a Show/Hide disclosure while the actions stay visible, and the Transaction type dropdown opens with Expense selected and no empty option.

**Spacing comes from tokens.** `src/ui-system.css` holds the sheet's scale - `--wvb-space-1` 4px to `--wvb-space-11` 60px on 4/6/8/10/12/16/20/24/30/32/60, the ink ramp `#0C0C0D` to `#A3AEB8`, `--wvb-surface-100` `#F4F7F9`, `--wvb-surface-200` `#E9ECEF`, `--wvb-line-400` `#DFE3E6`, the blues `#1279FF` / `#EAF3FF` / `#CDE2FD` and the 8/10/12 radii - and `--ui-bg`, `--ui-border` and `--ui-radius` now point at it. The dashboard and shared-card rhythm lives in the last block of `src/ui-quality-polish.css`, inside `@media(min-width:721px)` so the narrow-screen values stay: sections pad at 20, blocks sit 24 apart, rows pad 12/20. Take new spacing from these tokens. The register tables and feature forms were deliberately left on their own pinned values, because tests and `AGENTS.md` assert them.

**An organisation mark is a fixed-size tile.** `.erpNavigation button>span{flex:1;min-width:0}` outranks a bare `.wcMark` rule, so the trigger tile stretched to 95x32 and its 95px image was clipped by the sidebar's `overflow:hidden` - the fault the user saw. Keep `.wcTrigger>.wcMark{flex:0 0 var(--wc-mark-w,32px)}`, `.wcMenuGroup>.wcMenuRow>.wcMenuMark{flex:0 0 var(--wc-mark-w,28px)}`, `.wcMark,.wcMenuMark{position:relative;overflow:hidden}` and the absolutely filling image (`inset:0`, `object-fit:cover`, `border-radius:inherit`, `transform:scale(var(--wc-logo-scale,1))`). `src/demo-organisations.js` carries the per-company optical `logoScale` (Wayvida 1.35 for its wide white canvas) and `logoWide` for a wordmark, which widens the chip through `--wc-mark-w` (48px panel, 56px trigger) instead of squeezing a wordmark into a square.

Verified live at a temporary 1440x900 viewport, reset afterwards: trigger tile 32x32 with `flex:0 0 32px` and its image 43x43 clipped inside; Wayvida row 28x28 with `flex:0 0 28px`; Viskool row 48x28 with `flex:0 0 48px`; dashboard `metricGrid` gap 16px, `.metric` padding 20px, `.exactDash` padding `20px 24px 32px`, dashboard and body background `rgb(244, 247, 249)`. Full sweep **701 tests / 690 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures**; build and documentation validator pass.

The new logo file is `public/viskool-logo.png`.

**The contract that matters: ids are keys, names are display.** The working-context storage pair, the period-control records and the journal posting context all resolve through the organisation `id` ('abc', 'northstar'), the organisation `code` ('ABC01', 'NSR02') and the branch ids, so a company rename must move `name`, `logo`, branch `name` and branch `code` only. That is why Viskool's Trivandrum branch still carries the id `northstar-chennai`, and why the four old companies (and Calicut, Kannur, Mumbai, Pune) were deleted from the list rather than renamed into it. Kochi stays first in Wayvida because `src/organisation-context.js` falls back to the first branch of the first organisation and every module's `wayvida-demo-branch` fallback is the Kochi branch.

Renamed with the list, and to be kept in step if the companies change again: `PERIOD_ORGANISATIONS` and `PERIOD_BRANCHES` plus the period auto-create list in `src/period-locking.js`, `seededCompanies` / `seededBranches` in `src/App.jsx`, the company defaults in `src/settings-store.js`, the id-to-code maps in `src/invoice-engine.js` and `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx`, the `companySelect` / `branchSelect` labels and the welcome subtitle in `src/App.jsx`, and three sample place-name lists (a Chart of Accounts branch placeholder, and the branch options in `src/OperationalModules.jsx` and `src/Vendors.jsx`). Ten test files carried the old names in their assertions and were repointed.

The switcher renders the logos: the trigger shows the current company's 32px rounded-square logo (`span.wcMark` with an `<img>`, initials as the fallback), and the panel opens with an identity row before the ORGANISATION and BRANCH sections. An organisation row shows its 28px logo, the name over its type, and one right-hand slot - the tick on the current company, the `span.wcMenuBadge` branch count on the others. `object-fit:cover` is what makes the maroon Viskool square and the white-backed Wayvida glyph both read as app icons; keep it.

Verified live on the painted page in the in-app browser at a temporary 1440x900 viewport, reset afterwards, with the working context put back to Wayvida / Kochi Branch: the sidebar trigger read `Wayvida | Kochi Branch` with `/wayvida-logo.png` at x=14 / y=66 / w=241, `main` at y=59 / x=270 / w=1170, the panel identity row `Wayvida / Kochi Branch` with the same logo, the rows `Wayvida` (logo, ticked), `Viskool` (logo, `2 branches`), then `Kochi Branch KOC01` (ticked), `Trivandrum Branch TVM03` and `Bengaluru Branch BLR02`, and the footer row `Change / Customize`. Switching to Viskool landed on `Viskool | Trivandrum Branch`; Period Lock then opened on an empty register (Viskool holds no period records, which is the scope filter working) and Settings > Company listed only `Wayvida` and `Viskool`.

That last one is the accepted trade-off of putting the control inside the sidebar - do not add a second switcher to the header.

## Resume checkpoint — 2026-09-22 (the organisation and branch switcher)

On the user instruction "Company and Branch Section and switcher do like this ?" with a screenshot of a workspace switcher (a trigger with an identity mark and a chevron, a panel of options each carrying a check, and a final action row), the working-context bar's static read-out became a switcher. Read this before touching `src/HeaderOrgSelectors.jsx`, the new `src/org-switcher.js` or `src/header-org-selectors.css`.

What did **not** change, and must not: `div.orgContextBar` keeps its 46px height, its `left:270px` and its place below the 59px header (the `top:59px` / `height:46px` / `.app>main{margin-top:46px}` / `top:105px!important` geometry in `src/ui-quality-polish.css` is untouched), the `Change / Customize` drawer keeps its 540px right-side geometry, its two searches, `Select all`, `Apply Context`, its `role="checkbox"` rows and the `wcBox` tick, and the two change events still fire with the same detail shape.

`src/HeaderOrgSelectors.jsx` now renders `button.wcTrigger` (a 30px initials mark from `initialsOf`, the organisation name, the branch or `n branches`, and a 16px `IconChevronDown` that rotates through `.open`) inside `div.wcIdentity`, and while `menu` is set, `div.wcMenu` with `id="wc-switcher"`: an ORGANISATION section and a BRANCH section of `button.wcMenuRow` rows (`role="radio"`, `aria-checked`, a 28px `span.wcMenuMark` tone mark, `span.wcMenuText` with the name over `type · n branches` or the branch code, and a 20px `IconCircleCheckFilled` on the active row), then `i.wcMenuDivider` and the `button.wcMenuAction` row whose label is still `Change / Customize` and which calls `launch()`. One `useEffect` closes the panel on an outside `pointerdown` and on `Escape`, both ignored while the event target is inside `.wcIdentity` (the panel is a child of it). The trigger carries `aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded={menu}` and `aria-controls="wc-switcher"`.

`src/org-switcher.js` is the non-React half, and it exists so the quick switch and the drawer cannot write the working context differently. `applyWorkingContext(organizations, companyIds, branchIds, mode)` writes `wayvida-context-companies`, `wayvida-context-branches`, `wayvida-context-mode`, `wayvida-demo-company` and `wayvida-demo-branch`, dispatches `wayvida-organization-change` and `wayvida-working-context-change`, and returns the resolved `{company, branch}`; `switchedBranchIds(organization, branchIds)` keeps the branch the user is already on when it belongs to the chosen organisation and otherwise takes that organisation's first branch; `initialsOf` and `toneOf` build the marks; `SWITCH_MODES` is `{single: 'Single organisation', multi: 'Multi Selection'}`. `HeaderOrgSelectors` wraps that in one `commitContext` used by `apply()` (drafts, multi) and by `pickCompany` / `pickBranch` (one organisation or branch, then the panel closes). Do not write those keys anywhere else, and do not rename the modes: the tests pin both.

A row mark may only use blue, violet, cyan or amber. Green, red and grey already mean status in the Chart of Accounts, so a green organisation avatar would read as a state change; the tone is derived from the organisation id, not from its name, so it never shuffles.

Verified live in the in-app browser at a temporary 1440x900 viewport on the dev server at port 4001, then the viewport was reset and the working context was put back to ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd / Kochi Branch. The bar measured 46px tall at x=270 with the panel 340px wide at x=294 and its top 5px below the bar; the four organisation rows and the three ABC branch rows rendered the checks on ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd and Kochi Branch; choosing Northstar Retail LLP landed on Chennai Branch and survived a reload; the footer row closed the panel and opened the drawer with `Northstar Retail LLP` and `Chennai Branch` selected; `Escape` cleared `aria-expanded` and an outside click closed the panel; at 503px the subtitle is hidden, the trigger measures 235px and the panel still fits (`left:12`, `right:352`).

## Resume checkpoint — 2026-09-22 (the navigation sidebar toggle)

On the user instruction "add navigation / sidebar toggle" the dashboard shell gained one desktop collapse control. Read this before touching `src/App.jsx` or `src/navigation.css`, because the shell offset is duplicated across several other stylesheets and the toggle has to reach every one of them.

`src/App.jsx` owns one `nav` state seeded from `localStorage['finance-erp-nav-collapsed']` (`'1'` means collapsed), a `toggleNav` handler that writes the same key, and one effect that mirrors the state onto `document.body` as the `navCollapsed` class. The `.app` root renders `className={'app'+(nav?' navCollapsed':'')}`, the sidebar is `<aside id="appNav" ...>`, and the header's first child is `button.navToggle` carrying `aria-label` / `aria-expanded` / `aria-controls="appNav"` plus the `IconLayoutSidebarLeftCollapse` / `IconLayoutSidebarLeftExpand` glyphs at 19px, placed before the mobile `button.hamb`. The mobile drawer state (`menu` / `.open`) is untouched and independent of it.

`src/navigation.css` holds the whole geometry in one `@media(min-width:701px)` block: `.app.navCollapsed:has(>aside .erpNavigation){padding-left:0}`, `.app.navCollapsed>header{left:0}` and `.app.navCollapsed>aside{transform:translateX(-100%);visibility:hidden}`. The `visibility:hidden` is load-bearing - it is what takes the slid-out sidebar out of the tab order - and the breakpoint scope is load-bearing too, because an unscoped `.app.navCollapsed>aside{transform:...}` would outrank the mobile `aside.open{transform:none}` and lock the drawer shut. The button itself is `display:none` by default and revealed only inside that block as `.app>header>button.navToggle:not(.primary):not(.hamb){display:grid}`; the `:not()` pair is required because the compact-header rule `header>button:not(.primary):not(.hamb){display:none}` in `src/styles.css` hides every secondary header button below 1050px.

The 270px sidebar offset is not one value in one place. Besides `.app` and `.app>header` in `src/navigation.css`, it also sits on `.orgContextBar` (`src/ui-quality-polish.css`), `.pc` (`src/period-closing-focus.css`), `.transactionPortal` (`src/transaction-register.css`), `.settingsPortal` / `.helpPortal` / `.auditPortal` (`src/settings-portal.css`, `src/help-center.css`, `src/audit-log.css`, plus the `:where()` block in `src/ui-quality-polish.css`), the item create action bar (`.itemCreatePage .itemDialogFooter`, `src/items.css`) and the guided drawer backdrop (`.guideBackdrop`, `src/contextual-help.css`). Every one of those is portalled to `document.body`, so they cannot read the React state; they read the body class instead: `body.navCollapsed :where(.orgContextBar,.auditPortal,.settingsPortal,.helpPortal,.pc,.transactionPortal){left:0}`, one more rule for the item action bar, and one for the guide backdrop that needs `!important` because that drawer sheet sets its own inset with `!important`. Do not refactor those literals into a custom property unless you are ready to re-point every sheet and the tests that pin them.

Verified live in the in-app browser at 1440x900: expanded measures header `left:270px`, `.app` `padding-left:270px`, `.orgContextBar` `left:270px` and main at x=270 / w=1170; collapsed measures header `left:0`, `padding-left:0`, bar `left:0` and main at x=0 / w=1440, with the sidebar at `translateX(-100%)` and `visibility:hidden`. Both states survive a reload, and the narrow layout is unaffected: below 701px no collapse rule applies, the sidebar stays off-canvas until `.open`, and the toggle is `display:none` so the hamburger keeps owning that breakpoint. `tests/navigation-toggle-ui.test.mjs` pins the markup, the persistence key, the desktop-only scoping and each reclaimed offset.

## Resume checkpoint — 2026-09-21 (global sticky column headings and the header hand-over)

Every table page now shares one scroll behaviour, and it lives entirely in `src/register-head.js` plus `src/register-head.css`. Read this before adding a table page or touching a register stylesheet. The module finds the page grid - the first table in the scroll port with a `thead`, excluding `form,dialog,[role=dialog],details,.ivOverlay,.ivDetailSheet,.ivDetailBody,.dpPaper,.jp-page,.jp-sheet,.ivCreateForm,.itemCreatePage,.soCreatePage,.budgetWizard,.am-create-drawer` - marks it `data-sticky-head`, and publishes the page heading row height as `--register-head-h`, republished as `0px` while that row is hidden; the stylesheet pins `[data-sticky-head] thead th` at `top:var(--register-head-h,0px)` with `z-index:6`, the `inset 0 -1px 0` border stand-in and a `.22s` top transition. Do not declare a fill there: every shell paints its `th` opaquely and a sticky cell must stay opaque.

The trap is scroll containers. Sticky positions against the nearest scrolling ancestor, so a heading inside `.ivScroll` or `.itemsTableWrap` (both `overflow:auto`) would pin to a box that never moves. The module releases that box to `overflow:visible` through a `stickyHeadWrap` class **only while the table fits it horizontally**, and re-decides on resize - the sales orders register, whose table is `min-width:920px`, pins at 1440 and keeps its sideways scrolling at 900 and 760. Never release a wrapper unconditionally.

The hide threshold is `HIDE_AFTER=48` plus the row own height, because hiding a partly visible row at its natural position reads as a jump, and a `focusin` handler clears the hidden state so a keyboard-reached page action is never left off screen. Verified live across fourteen pages: merged registers pin at their own 76-80px row height, the four not-yet-merged purchase and payment registers pin at 0px, and Chart of Accounts still computes its own 65px so its bespoke `--am-coa-bar-h` implementation is untouched. On the invoices register the hand-over was measured in all three states (visible at 76px, hidden at 0px, restored on upward scroll) with the table x, width and row height unchanged throughout. `tests/sticky-table-head.test.mjs` pins all of it; the full sweep is 717 tests / 706 passing / the same 11 pre-existing failures.

## Resume checkpoint — 2026-09-21 (filter icon parity, invoice split menu, one sales order row action)

Three user requests landed together and each has a trap worth carrying forward. Read this before touching `src/sales-orders.css`, `src/register-head.css`, `src/SalesOrders.jsx`, `src/SalesOrderActions.jsx` or `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx`.

The filter icon on Sales Orders and Sales Invoices was reported as different from the one on Customers and Chart of Accounts. It was not: every register mounts `IconFilter` at `size={17}`. Measured, the funnel rendered 17x17 on Customers, Journal Entries, Chart of Accounts, Vendors, Items and Inventory Adjustments but 8.5x17 on the invoices register and 5.3x17 on the sales orders register, because flex shrinking reached the glyph inside the disclosure - the button label is `white-space:nowrap` and cannot shrink, so the svg absorbed it. `.soFiltersMore` in `src/sales-orders.css` was the only filter disclosure in the app without `flex:none`, which `.customerFilters`, `.iaFilters` and `.budgetFiltersMore` all carry. It is `position:relative;flex:none;margin-left:auto` now, and `src/register-head.css` guards every register with `.registerHead details:has(>summary[aria-label="Open filters"]){flex:0 0 auto}` and `.registerHead summary[aria-label="Open filters"]>svg{flex:0 0 auto;width:17px;height:17px}`. A glyph narrower than it is tall is a shrink, never a different icon; measure the rendered box before changing an icon. Verified at 1440, 1280, 1100, 1000, 900, 820 and 700px with no horizontal overflow and 17x17 everywhere.

The Sales invoice split menu lost `Use demo example` and `Export` on request, so it now holds `Account mapping` alone and the split stays because that is still a real secondary action. `exportRegister` went with them, along with the `IconDownload` import, the `csvReport` / `downloadReport` import from `src/daybook-export.js` and every use of `wayvida-invoices.csv`. Do not restore them. No Import was added: the invoice engine has no import contract.

The Sales orders register now has one row action, `View details`, which opens the invoice the order produced in the Invoices module through `wayvida-open-invoice` plus `onNavigate('Invoices')` - the same handoff Credit Notes and Day Book use - resolving the invoice as `convertSalesOrder` does (`order.invoice.id`, then the invoice store by `sourceOrder`). Per-row `Edit` and `Preview` are gone, `.soPreviewButton` is deleted from `src/sales-orders.css`, and the Actions column drops from 17% to 13% with Customer 10.5% to 12.5% and Branch 14% to 16% in the live width block and the matching adjustments in the superseded block above it. Critically, the row menu had never carried editing, so `Edit order` was added as its first entry - `SalesOrderActions` now takes `onEdit`, on the exact condition the removed button was disabled by (`!!order.invoice || Completed || Cancelled`). Removing a row button without checking where else the action lives would have deleted the capability. An order with no invoice yet is disabled with the reason in its title rather than navigating nowhere, because the register has no order detail screen of its own.

The account list also shows one section at a time: `AccountWorkspace` holds `const [openGroup,setOpenGroup]=useState(')` and resolves `const openType=typeGroups.some(group=>group.name===openGroup)?openGroup:(typeGroups[0]?.name||')` over the types that actually have rows, so the first visible group is open by default, a filter that hides the chosen group falls back to the first visible one, and a search matching only one type opens that type; `AccountGroup` takes `openGroup` / `setOpenGroup` instead of the old `type:X` key in `collapsed`, its toggle calls `setOpenGroup(type)`, and the `collapsed` array still owns the sub-account chevrons. A third pass the same day turned Add Account into the split button from the supplied reference, on the instruction "Use this Split button in add account and here give import and export": the two quiet icon buttons are gone and Import and Export now sit in the chevron dropdown. `div.am-split` holds `button.primary.am-split-main` beside `details.am-split-more`, whose 38px `summary` carries the 18px `IconChevronDown` and whose panel holds Import and Export. The primary half keeps `border-top-right-radius:0` / `border-bottom-right-radius:0` and the chevron half carries the outer `7px` radius and a `#6c9bf9` divider, so the two read as one 40px control. When writing any such panel, note that a plain `display` rule on a `<details>` child outranks the user-agent rule that hides closed content - the panel therefore declares `display:flex` only under `[open]`. Two things broke the page before they were caught by reloading rather than by the build: `IconChevronDown` was missing from the `@tabler/icons-react` import, and - earlier in the pass - a patch had replaced `const shellRef=useRef(null)` and another had deleted the component `return` line. A seventh pass the same day started the register-header rollout on the instruction to apply the Chart of Accounts header treatment to every grid and table page. Read this before migrating the next page: `src/register-head.css` and `src/register-head.js` are imported and installed once from `src/main.jsx` after `ui-quality-polish.css`, and every rule and query is scoped to a `registerHead` marker class so a page keeps its old header until it is migrated - the rollout is deliberately incremental and the app is never left half-changed. The stylesheet turns `div.ivHeading.registerHead` (or the `itemsHeading` equivalent) into one sticky row: the title and subtitle sit in `div.registerHeadText`, the page toolbar follows it with its card chrome stripped, and the search keeps the pair with the Filters disclosure because `margin-left:auto` sits on the search, not on the filter menu. The script installs one capture-phase document scroll listener - a scroll event does not bubble and the shell scroll port may not exist when the module loads - which hides the row while the reader scrolls down, returns it on the first upward scroll or at the very top, and publishes the measured height as `--register-head-h`. `src/Vendors.jsx` is the pilot: its `div.ivTools` moved out of `div.ivCard.ivRegisterCard` into the heading. Two things to know before continuing: the pages split across two shells with Budgets, Period Lock, Journal Entries and Inventory Adjustments on bespoke headers, and Goods Receipts, Purchases and Debit Notes each carry an inner `ivHeading` inside their create form ("Items to receive", "Items and services"), so the register heading must be located by hand on every page. Two mechanisms are load-bearing and easy to break. First, `.am-coa-page .am-table-wrap:has(.am-account-grid)` is `overflow:visible;max-height:none;overscroll-behavior:auto` - the wrapper is not a scroll port any more, `.app>main` is the page scroller, and below 700px only the wrapper regains `overflow-x:auto` while the sticky cells go static. Second, because the column header row now sticks against `main`, it offsets by the measured bar height: the effect in `AccountWorkspace` publishes `bar.offsetHeight` as `--am-coa-bar-h` (65px fallback) and watches it with a `ResizeObserver`, so `thead th` is `sticky;top:var(--am-coa-bar-h);z-index:6` and drops by the bar height under `.am-coa-bar-hidden`. That class is toggled from the shell scroll direction and applies `transform:translateY(calc(-100% - 32px))`; the extra 32px clears the shell padding, without which the lower 22px of the bar stayed visible. `IconAdjustments` must stay imported because the account-detail `toggle` action uses it, and `docs/AGENTS.md` (the repository `AGENTS.md`) now records the new scroll contract, replacing the old "bounded scroll port" wording. Unresolved: the bar hides only while scrolling down past its own height, so a short page never hides it - intended, not a defect.

The customer card carries six facts over two rows (tax treatment, GSTIN, PAN / contact person, phone, credit limit), all read from the customer record. The action bar pins Total amount and Total quantity on the left, computed from the same `calculate(form, config)` call the Billing summary uses so the two can never disagree, and null until the document can be calculated. Not adopted: the currency chip, "View Customer Details" (it would navigate away from an unsaved order) and a REMARKS block (notes and terms are in Additional details). Traps recorded: the action bar's own `justify-content:flex-end` beat the new `space-between` on source order so the override needed three classes; and the markup surgery left a stray `div>` text node in the customer card that only a full-resolution screenshot caught. Validation: sales-order-ui 42/42, invoice-detail-ui 15/15, full sweep 644 tests with 633 passing and the same 11 pre-existing failures, build and documentation validator passed. Three tests in sales-order-ui that had been nested inside another test were also un-nested.

The Customer and Order / Invoice details cards no longer stretch to a shared height. At 1440x950 both were 588px tall while the details card held only 304px of content - 214px of hollow white space. `.soOrderTop` is now `align-items:start` at `42fr / 58fr` with `.soOrderTop>.ivCard{align-self:start}`; after the change the Customer card is 540px and the details card 393px, neither with interior dead space. Seven fields in a two-column grid also left the last row half empty, so the odd field spans the row (`@media(min-width:1081px)` only - below 1080px the grid is four columns and the seventh is already in the flow). The three tax facts became one strip across the card - a 12px label over a 13px value in three columns, with the addresses on their own full-width rows - taking the facts block from 308px to 259px. Keep that strip rule at every width: scoped to the side-by-side layout only, the stacked five-track row squeezes each short fact to about 110px and wraps "Not recorded" one character per line. Superseded later the same day: the row is a 60/40 split with align-items:stretch restored, and the eight order fields pair two-by-two above the fold - see The create-page row split is 60/40 at the end of this section. Validation: sales-order-ui and invoice-detail-ui 55/55, four assertions rewritten, full sweep 642 tests with 631 passing and the same 11 pre-existing failures, build and documentation validator passed, verified in the browser at 1440, 1000 and 420px.

The place of supply on both sales documents is now derived from the addresses the app already holds, and the invoice inherits it from the order.

- The stored field is unchanged in name and meaning: `place`, the state-name string `calculate()` compares with the company state and every template prints. Two additive optional fields join it - `placeSource` (`customer_address` / `shipping_address` / `manual`) and `placeCode` (the GST state code from the existing `GST_STATE_CODES` table). Never rename `place`; a large amount of stored data and every reader depends on it.
- `src/customer-tax.js` owns the rules: `placeOfSupplySuggestion` (distinct shipping address, then the customer's registered place, then the state named in the billing text), `findStateInAddress` (only names the app knows, longest match wins, so a city never becomes a state), `placeOfSupplyStatus` (the helper line and the reset label) and `gstStateCode`.
- `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx` owns the interaction: `chooseCustomer` fills the three fields from the suggestion, `applyPlace(next, force)` recalculates only a derived value, and a stored document with a place but no source is treated as manual so old records are never silently re-taxed. Choosing a value by hand sets `placeSource:'manual'` and reveals `Reset to shipping state` / `Reset to customer state`. The control sits at the foot of the Customer card under the addresses (`div.soPlaceOfSupply`, `#soPlaceOfSupply`, `p.soPlaceHint`), not in the details grid - that grid is now seven fields. (Superseded later the same day: the control is the eighth field of the details grid, immediately right of the Due date, with its source line as a small `soPlaceHint` under it.)
- `convertSalesOrder` carries `place`, `placeSource` and `placeCode` to the invoice draft. Direct invoice creation derives through the same component. Posting is unchanged and still requires a place of supply; do not add GST maths to the UI - `calculate()` already decides CGST+SGST vs IGST from the final value.
- Validation: `tests/customer-tax.test.mjs` 19/19, `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` 40/40, `tests/sales-order.test.mjs` 5/5, full sweep 642 tests with 631 passing and the same 11 pre-existing failures, build and documentation validator passed. Verified in the browser end to end, including the tax flipping between CGST+SGST and IGST as the place of supply changed.
### Follow-up of the same day: create-page padding, action bar and narrow table

Three defects were found and fixed on the two shared create pages, all by measuring the running page.

- The form rules (`.soCreatePage .salesOrderForm`, `.ivCreatePage .ivCreateForm`) set `margin:12px 0 0`, a shorthand that resets all four margins. It tied on specificity with the shared shell gutter (`.soCreatePage>:not(.soCreateHead)`, both `0,2,0`) and won on source order, so neither create page had side padding - the invoice form measured `x:270, right:1425` at a 1440px viewport - and the action bar's deliberate `-24px` bleed landed at `right:1449`, overflowing the document by 24px. Both now set `margin-top` / `margin-bottom` only. Do not reintroduce a shorthand there.
- `.soCreatePage .soActionBar` was `position:sticky;bottom:0` and covered the Billing summary and its totals while the form was at the top. It is now `position:static`, an in-flow footer, with the container bottom padding reduced from `88px` to `28px`. (Superseded later on 2026-09-19: the bar is pinned again, and `.soBarActions{margin-left:auto}` keeps the actions on the right whether or not the running totals render - see the checkpoint at the end of this section.)
- The shared line table's `table-layout:fixed;min-width:0` squeezed the eight columns to about 35px below 700px, so a `@media(max-width:700px)` block sets `min-width:760px` and lets `.ivScroll` scroll it at phone width.
- Validation: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` and `tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` 53/53, full sweep 636 tests with 625 passing and the same 11 pre-existing failures, production build, `prepare-sites-build` and the documentation validator all passed. Measured at 1440x950 and 420x900: 24px gutters, no horizontal overflow, `overlap: false`.
- Still open, deliberately: the same sticky-footer overlap exists on Create Item and Create Journal Entry, which use the `.itemDialogFooter` / journal sticky-card pattern. It was outside this request.

The New / Edit sales invoice page is now the Create Sales Order page. Read this before touching `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx`, `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx`, `src/sales-orders.css` or `src/invoice-workspace.css`.

- `src/SalesDocumentFields.jsx` no longer branches its layout on `kind`. One shared block renders for both documents: `div.soOrderTop` with the Customer card (searchable `SearchSelect` picker, then the read-only GST treatment / GSTIN / PAN facts and the billing and shipping addresses with their labelled Edit) at 40% beside the document-details card at 60%, whose eight fields are one `soFieldRow4` four-column grid - organisation, branch, number, date, reference, place of supply, payment terms, due date. Wording switches through `docWord` / `docTitle`; do not reintroduce a `kind==='order'?<>` layout branch, or the two pages start drifting again.
- Only these differ by document, and they must stay different: the invoice keeps an editable Unit input (its engine refuses a line with no unit) while the order reads the unit from the Item master; the invoice keeps its `Item tax and price treatment are filled from the Item master.` note; the invoice keeps `Save draft` because it never posts from the create page; the order keeps its credit-limit notice (`overLimit` is still order-only) and its shipment / delivery / salesperson fields.
- The invoice adopts the shell by class, not by copy: `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` sets `const createOpen=!!form` and renders `invoiceWorkspace soCreatePage ivCreatePage`, so the head bar, the gutter, the fact list, the address editor, the Additional details card and the sticky action bar come from `src/sales-orders.css` by construction. `src/invoice-workspace.css` holds only the invoice page's own rules. If you add a shared-shell rule, put it in `src/sales-orders.css` under `.soCreatePage` so both pages get it at once.
- A new invoice is seeded with the working-context organisation and branch (`getCurrentOrganizationContext()` in `create()`), the way the order is seeded, and the save still lets a stored value win. Do not remove the pickers: both are required fields now.
- Do not restore the create page's inline `button.ivLink` back link or its `div.ivHeading` block; the head is `div.soCreateHead`. `ivHeading` itself is still live - the register and the other invoice screens use it through the `heading()` helper.
- Keep the `@media(max-width:560px)` block that restates the `.soOrderTop`-scoped fact-list and field-row folds. Without it, `.soCreatePage .soOrderTop .soFactList` (three classes) beats the two-class `@media(max-width:560px)` fold whatever the source order, because media queries add no specificity, and every read-only value wraps one character per line at 420px. This trap applies to any new `.soOrderTop`-scoped rule.
- Deliberately not included: document attachments on the invoice. The order stores `files` and has a drop zone plus a file list; the invoice has no attachment reader (preview, share, print), so this is a separate feature, not a styling gap.
- Validation for this checkpoint: `tests/sales-order-ui.test.mjs` 38/38, `tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` 15/15, full sweep 636 tests with 625 passing and the same 11 pre-existing failures, production build passed, `prepare-sites-build` and the documentation validator passed, and both pages were verified in the browser at 1440x950 and 420x900 including a draft save that posted nothing.

The Customers register toolbar was reworked and the Sales invoices register search was styled on 2026-09-19. Read this before touching either screen.

- `src/Customers.jsx` no longer renders the `{visible.length} customers` count. The page keeps the search box and the always-visible status select, and adds one `details.customerFilters` disclosure whose panel holds five `label.customerFilterField` selects - Type, State / place of supply, GST treatment, Payment terms and Balance - over one `Clear filters` action and a count badge derived from `CUSTOMER_FILTER_DEFAULTS` (`Object.values(filters).filter(value=>!value.startsWith('All ')).length`). Never reintroduce the count, never move the status select into the panel, and never build the badge from a second bookkeeping list.
- The filter predicates read stored fields - `type` (with the register's `Business` default), `state`, `gstTreatment`, `paymentTerms`, `status` - except Balance, which reads the `credit` summary the register already computes through `customerCreditSummary` and is therefore applied after that `.map()`. Nothing new is stored and no key was renamed.
- `src/customers.css` styles the panel on the Period Lock geometry and resets the shared toolbar label box with `.customersPage .itemsTools .customerFilterField` (`.itemsTools label` paints the bordered search pill at a fixed 340px width). Below 760px the toolbar wraps, the disclosure takes a full-width row and the panel becomes a static single-column block; without that rule the absolute panel anchored `right:0` to a trigger near the left edge of a narrow toolbar runs off the screen. Keep the page's single `pointerdown` / `Escape` effect closing `.customerKebab[open]` and `.customerFilters[open]` together - do not add a second listener.
- The Sales invoices register search in `src/InvoiceWorkspace.jsx` is now `label.ivToolSearch` holding `IconSearch` at 17px beside the input, styled in `src/invoice-workspace.css` so the wrapper owns the border, the radius and the `:focus-within` ring and the nested input gives up the shared input padding, border and background. The register query, the status filter and the columns are unchanged.
- Validation actually executed for this checkpoint: `node --test tests/customer-register-ui.test.mjs tests/invoice-detail-ui.test.mjs` is 23/23; the full sweep is 634 tests with 623 passing and the same 11 pre-existing failures recorded for 2026-09-18; the production build passed with 6,966 modules transformed; `node scripts/prepare-sites-build.mjs` and `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed; and both screens were verified in the in-app browser against the running dev server. The environment still needs escalated approval for commands that spawn child processes (esbuild, the Node test runner) and for network access, because the default sandbox blocks both with `spawn EPERM` / restricted network. `npm` itself is not on `PATH` in this shell - the bundled runtime is at `C:\Users\aswin\.cache\codex-runtimes\codex-primary-runtime\dependencies\node\bin\node.exe`, which runs `node node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js` and `node --test` directly.


The Create / Edit Customer form in `src/Customers.jsx` was simplified into a conditional, GST-ready page on 2026-09-18. Read this before touching the Sales module.

- The page shell is unchanged: `form.itemCreatePage` as a direct child of `section.itemsPage.customersPage`, one `div.itemDialogHead`, one `div.itemDialogBody` card and `footer.itemDialogFooter`, with no `<header>` element anywhere in the form.
- The body is three `section.itemSection` blocks in the order Customer details, GST & Tax Details, Billing address, then one collapsed `details.itemAdvanced` (More details) holding four groups: Payment & accounting, Opening balance & website, Contact persons and Documents. Always keep the create grid at `.customersPage .itemCreatePage .itemFormGrid` three columns (two below 1100px, one below 700px) with the `0 0 16px` margin on `.itemTypeField`, so the Customer type choice never touches the name row.
- `src/customer-tax.js` is the pure module behind both the form and the tests: `GST_TREATMENTS` with its metadata (`gstinApplicable`, `gstinLabel`, `panRequired`, `isBusinessTreatment`, `gstTreatmentMeta`), `decodeGstin` / `gstinChecksum`, `composeAddress` / `parseAddress`, `paymentTermDays` / `daysToPaymentTerm`, `normaliseContacts` / `setPrimaryContact` / `contactFromName` / `contactName` / `primaryContact`, `blankAddress` / `blankContact` / `blankCustomField` / `blankDocument`, `COUNTRIES`, `CURRENCIES`, `PAYMENT_TERMS`, `TAX_PREFERENCES`, and the `MAX_DOCUMENTS` / `MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` limits.
- The address is Address 1 and Address 2 (`line1` / `line2`) plus City, State, PIN code and Country. `parseAddress` puts the first line of a legacy composed address in Address 1 and everything after it in Address 2; never reintroduce the single Address textarea.
- Display name, Tags, Custom fields and Notes have no inputs by design. Do not delete their stored keys and do not stop writing them: `save` carries `displayName` (derived from the customer name when blank), `tags`, `notes` and `customFields` through untouched, so an existing customer keeps its data.
- The More details disclosure is controlled through `open={moreOpen}`. A failed save that errors on a field inside it must keep opening it, because the receivable account, credit limit, opening balance and contact people are all validated there.
- When editing the Sales Orders CSS, know two traps that only a rendered pass exposes. `src/items.css` styles `.itemsTools label` as the bordered search pill, so anything placed inside that toolbar inherits it - the filter panel therefore resets it with `.salesOrdersPage .soFiltersPanel label{…;padding:0;border:0;border-radius:0;width:auto;align-items:stretch}` at two-class specificity, because `.salesOrdersPage .itemsTools label` sets the width. And `.invoiceWorkspace button` (one class, one element) outranks a single-class rule, so every control the order panel adds is prefixed with `.soCreatePage` and the editor backdrop pins `background-color:…!important`, as the account drawer does.
