The Create Journal Entry page in `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` was redesigned on 2026-09-12 into a compact accountant workflow supported by two presentation-only modules: `src/journal-templates.js` and `src/JournalAccountPicker.jsx`, styled by the redesign section appended to `src/journal-form.css`.

The line grid has seven columns - Account, Organisation, Branch, Debit, Credit, Description and the row action - because organisation and branch are edited on each line between the account and debit columns. (Since the ninth pass below the grid renders seven, six or five columns, because those two columns appear only while the working context needs them.) `emptyLine()` still stamps `organization`, `branch` and `costCentre` from the working context, so `postToLedger()` continues to resolve `line.branch` and no engine contract changed.

Totals are labelled Total Debit and Total Credit, right-aligned under the Journal lines grid, and the balance status (`Debit equals credit`, or `Difference: <amount>`) now renders inside that same right-aligned block directly beneath those amounts. The Attach files button and its file-type hint sit at the left edge of the tools row, aligned with the lines grid, and Add Journal Line pairs with Use Template in the lines title row as a matching solid-border control.

Productivity features are Use Template (Monthly Rent, Salary Entry, Depreciation, Bank Transfer), save-as-template with a None/Every month/Every year repeat setting, and Duplicate Journal. Templates resolve an account by preferred code, then by name, and otherwise leave it blank rather than posting to a guessed account. Recurrence is stored on the template only; no scheduler and no automatic posting exists. Save draft, Post journal and a more-actions menu (Submit for approval, Duplicate, Reverse) live in the footer bar; Post stays disabled until the journal is approved, balanced and inside an open period, and the footer states the blocking reason. *(Superseded by the eighth pass below: the create-page footer now holds only the save action and Post journal, and Post gates on balanced totals inside an open period.)*

No accounting logic changed. `validate()`, `commit()`, `action()`, `audited()`, `postToLedger()`, the `wayvida-manual-journals-v2` record shape, the ledger engine and period locking are untouched; the only new storage is `wayvida-journal-templates-v1` and `wayvida-journal-account-prefs-v1`, which nothing in accounting reads. `tests/journal-templates.test.mjs` (8 passing) and `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` (11 passing) cover the new behaviour.

Follow-up refinement (2026-09-12, second pass): the two read-only Organisation/Branch boxes became one compact **Posting To** card whose in-place `Change` selector writes the existing `wayvida-context-companies`, `wayvida-context-branches`, `wayvida-context-mode`, `wayvida-demo-company` and `wayvida-demo-branch` keys, dispatches the existing working-context events, and re-stamps `organization` and `branch` on every line so the header stays authoritative. The five posting-condition badges collapsed into a single balance status line (`Journal is balanced` or `Difference: <amount>`) with the precise blocking condition kept as the tooltip and the footer still stating why Post journal is disabled. Additional details now also show read-only approval comments from the existing audit trail. A separate persisted Notes field was deliberately not added because it would put a new key into the stored journal record, which invariant 16 forbids - this is the one open decision. `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` now reports 16 passing tests and `tests/journal-templates.test.mjs` 8. The production build could not run this session (sandbox esbuild directory denial plus a misconfigured escalation reviewer), so every changed source was validated with `esbuild.transform()` instead.

Follow-up layout refinement (2026-09-12, third pass): the metadata row now holds Date, Journal type, Reference number and Reason on one line, with the reason as a plain single-line input bound to `narration`, and the generated-number hint removed. Organisation and branch moved into the line grid between the Account and Debit columns as per-line selects that store the same names `emptyLine()` stamped and `postToLedger()` reads, with the Posting To card kept as the default for new lines. The attachments showcase was replaced by one Attach files button, and the validation display now shows exactly four conditions (debit equals credit, date is valid, period is open, accounts are active) with `postingChecks()` reduced from five conditions to four. `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` reports 18 passing tests and `tests/journal-templates.test.mjs` 8; the full suite is 265 tests with 250 passing and only the same 15 pre-existing failures.
Fourth pass (2026-09-12): the Posting To card was removed from the Create Journal Entry page along with its Change popover, the `applyPostingContext` handler, `postingRef` and the `draftCompany`/`draftBranch` state, because organisation and branch are already editable on every line; the `.je-posting-*` CSS went with it. The `je-lock-alert` banner was also removed - a locked or unconfigured accounting period is now reported inline inside the date field (`je-date-locked` plus a small inline unlock action). `checkPeriod()`, `validate()`, `postingChecks()`, the seven-column grid, the four posting conditions and the stored journal shape are unchanged.
Fifth pass (2026-09-12): the date, period and account check chips were deleted from the Create Journal Entry page, leaving the balance status (`Debit equals credit` / `Difference`) as the only posting indicator; `postingChecks()` still returns the four conditions as the gate and the status tooltip now shows only the blocking detail. The inline lock hint is gated on genuinely locked periods (`dateLocked`), so open dates show just `Accounting period: <name>` and no period error. The Attach files button and its selected-file chips moved from the Additional details disclosure into the tools row beside `Add Journal Line`; the disclosure now holds approval comments only and renders only when they exist. No accounting logic, storage key or record field changed.
Sixth pass (2026-09-14): on the Create Journal Entry page the balance status (`Debit equals credit` / `Difference`) now sits inside the same right-aligned block as the Total Debit and Total Credit amounts, directly beneath them; the Attach files button and its file-type hint are left-aligned with the lines grid and lead with the button; and the Add Journal Line button beside Use Template uses the same solid-border treatment so the two read as one action pair. No accounting logic, storage key or record field changed. `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` reports 19 passing tests (18 previous plus one new footer-layout contract) against 8 for `tests/journal-templates.test.mjs`, with `journal-actions-ui` 2/2, `journal-entries-ui` 1/1 and `journal-detail-actions` 2/2. The three known-stale journal suites are unchanged. The production build could not run because this environment blocks esbuild child processes, and browser access to the local preview was auto-denied, so the change was validated by parsing the JSX with `@babel/parser` plus a CSS brace check and still needs a visual confirmation.
Correction (2026-09-14, after the sixth pass): the local preview blocker described above was resolved - `node tmp/dev-live.mjs` serves the edited sources at `http://localhost:5173/`, and the served module and stylesheet were verified to contain the new balance, attachment and button contracts (see docs/TESTING-AND-QA.md). The production build remains unavailable here.
Seventh pass (2026-09-14): the Create Journal Entry footer is now a single row. The Attach files button, its file-type hint and the selected-file chips keep the left edge of the lines grid, the optional Remove empty lines action follows them, and the Total Debit / Total Credit totals with the balance status close the same row on the right, so the previous tools row and the 52px balance block no longer stack. `je-lines-tools` was replaced by `je-lines-footer`; no calculation, validation, storage key or record field changed. `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` reports 19 passing tests and `tests/journal-templates.test.mjs` 8, with the three known-stale journal suites unchanged. The running preview at `http://localhost:5173/` serves the new markup and stylesheet; no rendered visual pass or production build was possible here.

Eighth pass (2026-09-14): the Create Journal Entry page loses its approval detour and its leftover controls. `canPost` is now `difference===0 && totals.debit>0 && periodCheck.allowed` - the extra requirement that the stored record already carry an approved status was what left Post journal disabled on a correctly balanced draft, and `validate()` no longer rejects an unapproved record either. Posting still refuses a locked or unconfigured period through `assertOpenPeriod()`, `postingChecks()` still returns the four conditions used as the status tooltip, and the record shape and ledger contract are unchanged. The balance status (`Debit equals credit` / `Difference: <amount>`) now stacks on its own line directly beneath the Total Debit and Total Credit amounts inside the same right-aligned footer block, which is now a column with a 2px gap. The Cancel button, the `Approve this journal before posting.` hint and the three-dot More actions disclosure (Submit for approval, Duplicate Journal, Reverse Journal) were removed from the create page along with the `.je-post-hint` and `.je-more-actions` rules. Because drafts had no other submit path, the approval entry point moved to the journal detail screen: a Draft, non-automatic journal shows a `Submit for approval` secondary action that sets Pending Approval and stamps `submittedBy`/`submittedAt`, after which the existing Approve / Reject / Post actions continue to work. A balanced journal can therefore now be posted straight from the create page without an intervening approval - a deliberate workflow change. `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` reports 20 passing tests and `tests/journal-templates.test.mjs` 8, with `journal-detail-actions` 2/2, `journal-actions-ui` 2/2 and `journal-entries-ui` 1/1; the three known-stale journal suites are unchanged. The running preview at `http://localhost:5173/` serves this markup and stylesheet; no rendered visual pass or production build was possible here.

Ninth pass (2026-09-14): the Journal lines Organisation and Branch columns are now conditional on the working context that the header selector writes (`wayvida-context-companies` / `wayvida-context-branches`, read through the existing `getAccessibleOrganizations()`). One accessible organisation hides the Organisation column; one organisation with a single branch hides Branch as well, so the grid renders five columns and posts implicitly to that organisation and branch; one organisation with several branches keeps Branch only (six columns); several organisations keep both (seven columns). The rules are `showOrganizationColumn=contextOrganizations.length>1` and `showBranchColumn=showOrganizationColumn||(contextOrganizations[0]?.branches?.length||0)>1`, implemented with the `no-organization` / `no-branch` row classes and three grid templates restricted to `min-width: 761px` so the mobile card layout is unchanged. A new `alignLine()` helper plus a guarded `useEffect` keep every line on an organisation and branch that still exist in the context: the organisation falls back to the first accessible one and the branch is kept only while it belongs to that organisation, so changing an organisation clears an incompatible branch. `lineBranchOptions(line)` now returns `branchOptionsFor(line.organization)` alone, so a branch belonging to another organisation can no longer appear in the dropdown. When a column is hidden the Journal lines subtitle reports the implicit choice (`Posting to <organisation>`, plus the branch when both are hidden) instead of adding a second selector. Amount alignment moved from `nth-child` position selectors to the `je-head-amount` and `je-cell-amount` classes so a hidden column cannot shift it. No calculation, validation rule, posting rule, storage key or stored record field changed. `tests/journal-create-ui.test.mjs` reports 22 passing tests and the new `tests/journal-line-columns.test.mjs` 6 passing behavioural scenarios that run the extracted page expressions against the three configurations plus empty, unavailable and full-list contexts, with `journal-templates` 8/8, `journal-detail-actions` 2/2, `journal-actions-ui` 2/2 and `journal-entries-ui` 1/1; the three known-stale journal suites are unchanged. The dev preview at `http://localhost:5173/` serves the new markup and stylesheet; the in-app browser was rejected by the environment's auto-review, so no rendered visual pass or production build was possible here.

### Create Budget account selection checkpoint - 2026-09-15

The Account Selection step of Create Budget now reads as the Chart of Accounts itself. `AccountSelectGrid` in `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` previously grouped rows by `account.group||account.type` and filtered the offered list through `TYPES[form.type]` / `BUDGET_TYPES`; because the `Budget type` field had been removed from step one, `form.type` kept its `'Profit & Loss Budget'` default, so the step silently offered Income and Expenses accounts only and a section heading could mix an account group such as `Operating Income` with a raw type. The wizard list is now `accounts.filter(account=>account.active&&!account.isGroup)` - every active posting account - and the search matches name, code, account group and type together. The grid is two columns: one `budgetGroupRow` per accounting type in the shared `ACCOUNT_TYPE_ORDER` order (`Assets`, `Liabilities`, `Equity`, `Income`, `Expenses`, with an `Other` bucket so an unrecognised type is never dropped), each row named by `accountTypeLabel(type,business)` and carrying its count (`n of m selected`, or `m accounts`), then one row per account whose `budgetAccountCell` prints the account name over its code and, when it differs from the type, its account group. The separate `Account Type` column and its `const columns=business?3:4` are gone, and the header reads `Account` / `Money category`. The one accounting-type vocabulary now lives in `src/terminology.jsx`: export `ACCOUNT_TYPE_ORDER` and `accountTypeLabel(type,business)`, which returns the raw type for the accountant view and the existing business wording (`Assets` -> `What You Own`, `Expenses` -> `Money Spent`) for the business view, so the budget step and the Chart of Accounts page cannot disagree; do not add a second type-label map to either page. In `src/budget-workspace.css` the type row is `tr.budgetGroupRow>*` (the spanning `th`) with a `display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:space-between` `div.budgetGroupCell` inside it (bold type, muted 12px count) and `.budgetAccountCell` is `padding:0` around a `label` that stacks the name over a 12px muted `small`. Never put `display:flex` on the `td`/`th` itself: a cell whose `display` is not `table-cell` leaves the table layout algorithm, and the running preview showed the type name wrapping one letter per line inside the 56px choose column - the flex row must live in `.budgetGroupCell` / the account `label`. This pass also fixed accessibility on that grid: an `aria-label` on the table, `th scope="col"` headers, a `th scope="colgroup" colSpan={2}` per type, a stable `budget-account-<code>` id per checkbox that its `label htmlFor` points at (so the whole name and code block is clickable and correctly named), an 18px checkbox with a 2px `#245fd9` `:focus-visible` ring, a `tbody tr:focus-within` tint, and `#667085` for the 12px code line instead of the lighter grey that measured 3.66:1. Only the unused `TYPES` lookup was removed from the `./budget-store.js` import; `BUDGET_TYPES` must stay imported because `BudgetList` and `BudgetReports` still build their `Budget type` filters from it - dropping it raised `ReferenceError: BUDGET_TYPES is not defined` in `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs`, which no unit suite reproduced.

Validation: `node tmp/dump-budget-accounts.mjs` renders both the accountant and business views with their sections, counts and code lines; `node tmp/budget-create-ssr.mjs` passes with the new expectations, including that asset accounts are offered and the sections follow chart order; `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/coa-detail-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/probe-scope-fix.mjs` all report OK; `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` reports `50/50 jsx files transformed` with the same five pre-existing notices; `tests/budget-create-flow-ui.test.mjs` 8/8; the full sweep reports `FILES=71 green=57 tests=400 passed=389 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)`, identical to the baseline. `node tmp/zscope2-ssr.mjs` now fails on a regenerated scratch probe (`tmp/pc2-probe.jsx` double-exports `CurrentPeriodPanel`), a pre-existing defect in an unrelated scratch script. No painted browser pass and no production build were performed, because in-app browser automation is blocked by this environment's review policy and `npm` is absent while esbuild cannot spawn here; the dev server serves the edited modules at `http://localhost:5173/` and the preview needs a hard reload.

### Create Budget scope universe and details grid checkpoint - 2026-09-15

The Create Budget page could not be scoped. `BudgetWizard` read its organisation list from `getAccessibleOrganizations()`, the working context the header is narrowed to, so with the header on one organisation and one branch that list resolved to a single organisation with a single branch, `getScopeVisibility()` reported `showOrganisation:false` / `showBranch:false`, `scopePickerRows()` returned no rows, and the `Budget scope` row rendered an empty `div.pc-scope-picker` beside the sentence `Planning for ABC Technologies Pvt Ltd · Kochi Branch.` - there was no field to open and no way to widen the scope.

The fix keeps one scope engine and gives the page the wider universe that engine needs. `src/organisation-context.js` now holds both lists behind one `scopedOrganisations(companyKey,branchKey,companyFallback,branchFallback)` derivation: `getAccessibleOrganizations()` (the narrowed working context, unchanged: `wayvida-context-companies` / `wayvida-context-branches`) and `getScopeOrganisations()` (every organisation and branch the header may offer: `wayvida-accessible-organizations` / `wayvida-accessible-branches`, falling back to the whole demo list so the universe is never empty). The Change / Customize drawer in `src/HeaderOrgSelectors.jsx` now reads that same shared reader instead of deriving the list inline, so the header and Create Budget cannot disagree about what may be offered. Nothing else was added: no third reader, no page-local copy, and no new `multi` flag in `getScopeVisibility()` / `scopePickerState()`, so Journal Lines, Period Lock and Chart of Accounts keep the exact behaviour they had.

`src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` reads the universe into state, seeds the opening scope from the working context through the one `wizardScopeSeed(initial)` helper, stores `companyIds` / `branchIds` on the budget's `scope` so editing reopens the scope the budget was saved with (legacy budgets without refs fall back to the working context), and re-reads the universe on `wayvida-working-context-change`, `wayvida-organization-change` and `storage`, pruning selections the new list no longer contains with `normaliseScope()`.

`src/budget-workspace.css` turns step one into one aligned form: `budgetSetupFields` is a four-column track (`repeat(4,minmax(0,1fr))`) where `budgetFieldWide` spans two columns and the financial year and budget period take one each, and the scope row reuses that track at `grid-column:span 2` per field so Organisation lines up under the budget name; the tracks fold to two columns below 1100px and one below 700px. `.budgetWizard .budgetSetup{overflow:visible}` keeps the open checkbox list from being clipped by the card, the same fix the journal lines card uses for the account combobox.

Validation: `node tmp/probe-scope-fix.mjs` shows the before and after state; `node tmp/budget-create-ssr.mjs` passes with the new expectations; `node tmp/dump-step1.mjs` prints the new markup; `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` reports `50/50 jsx files transformed`; `node tmp/ra2-profile-menu.mjs` reports `PROFILE MENU + DRAWER SSR OK`; `node tmp/budget-nav-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/bw-register-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/coa-detail-ssr.mjs` and `node tmp/zscope2-ssr.mjs` all pass; the full sweep reports `FILES=71 green=57 tests=400 passed=389 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)`, identical to the baseline. No painted browser pass and no production build were performed, because in-app browser automation is blocked by this environment's review policy and `npm` is absent while esbuild cannot spawn here; the dev server serves the edited modules at `http://localhost:5173/` and needs a hard reload.

### Budget create flow checkpoint - 2026-09-15

On the user instruction the Create Budget page became three steps. `BudgetWizard` in `src/BudgetWorkspace.jsx` now renders `Budget Details`, `Account Selection` and `Budget Allocation`; the separate `Review Budget` step is gone and its totals moved onto the allocation step. Step one keeps one `budgetSetupFields` grid (Budget name, Financial year, Budget period, Budget type) and adds a `budgetScopeRow` rendering the shared `OrganisationBranchScope` over `getAccessibleOrganizations()`, with the visibility, branch list and branch clearing coming from `scopePickerState()` in `src/organisation-scope.js`; the wizard keeps `companyIds` / `branchIds` on the form and derives the stored scope from the picker state, so `BUDGET_SCOPES`, the hardcoded branch list and the manual scope select are gone from that page (the register scope filter still reads `BUDGET_SCOPES`). Step two renders the new `AccountSelectGrid`: one `.budgetAccountGrid` table grouped by `account.group||account.type` with a labelled checkbox per row and no account-type tabs (`AccountTree` stays for the detail Accounts tab). Step three allocates and saves with `Create Budget` (`Save Changes` when editing). Because `Save Draft` and `Submit for Approval` left the create page, `BudgetDetail` gained one mode-aware status action (`Submit for approval`, `Approve budget`, `Activate budget`, `Mark complete`) wired to `setBudgetStatus` and the engine `FLOW`, and an edit keeps the stored status instead of resetting it to `Draft`. No storage key, calculation, allocation, revision, variance, forecast or settings behaviour changed. ### Chart of Accounts sticky grid head checkpoint - 2026-09-15

The Chart of Accounts page now keeps its filter row and its table head visible while the account list scrolls, on the user instruction that the Search / Type / Status / Filters row, the column header row (Account Name, Account Code, Account Group, Status, Actions) and the account-group accordion row (Assets, Liabilities, Equity, Income, Expenses) should be sticky. The change is CSS-only and appended to `src/account-workspace.css`; no component, filter, tree or drawer file changed. Superseded on 2026-09-21: the wrapper is no longer a bounded scroll port and the account-group accordion row no longer exists, so read the Chart of Accounts header, page scroll and grid checkpoint above for the current contract.

Two facts decide the implementation. First, the shell keeps the working content as the only scroll port: `html,body,#root{height:100%;overflow:hidden}`, `.app{height:100dvh;overflow:hidden}` and `.app>main{height:calc(100dvh - 105px);overflow-x:clip;overflow-y:auto}` live in `src/ui-quality-polish.css`, which `src/main.jsx` imports last, so `.am-coa-page>.am-filter-toolbar{position:sticky;top:0;z-index:9}` pins the filter row directly with no header offset to maintain. Second, the account grid already had to scroll horizontally (`.am-account-grid{min-width:1120px}` inside `.am-table-wrap:has(.am-account-grid){overflow:auto}`), and a scroll port with an unconstrained height sticks nothing vertically; the new rule gives that wrapper a bound (`max-height:max(360px,calc(100vh - 280px))` with a `dvh` line beside it as the fallback and `overscroll-behavior:contain`), which turns it into the grid's own vertical scroller and lets the header row (`position:sticky;top:0;z-index:6`, keeping the opaque `#f7f9fc` cell fill) and the group row (`position:sticky;top:calc(var(--am-coa-head-h) - 1px);z-index:5`, keeping `#edf2f9`) stick inside it, under the single `--am-coa-head-h:40px` header measurement. Inset shadows replace the collapsed-table border that browsers drop from sticky cells, and the one-pixel group-row overlap hides any measurement drift behind the header.

Nothing else changed, so no contract moved: the register, the tree, `colSpan` arithmetic, `scopeColumns`, the empty state, the Create/Edit Account drawer, `.am-layout.has-detail` and `.am-detail{position:sticky;top:82px}` are untouched, and no test asserted CSS geometry before. The one new file is `tests/account-sticky-grid-ui.test.mjs`, a five-assertion CSS contract for the pinned filter row, the bounded grid wrapper (both `max-height` lines, `overscroll-behavior:contain`, never `overflow:visible`) and the two sticky rows under the row menu.

### Organisation and branch picker checkpoint - 2026-09-14

The organisation/branch rule now has one interactive control as well as one implementation. `src/OrganisationBranchScope.jsx` renders from the pure `scopePickerState({organisations,companyIds,branchIds,label})` helper added to `src/organisation-scope.js`, so the selectors, the branch options and the cleared incompatible branch are identical wherever the picker appears; it imports only `ALL_BRANCHES`, `normaliseScope` and `scopePickerState`, keeps no `flatMap` branch filtering, and resolves a one-option branch automatically so no single-option selector is ever rendered.

The Request Unlock form renders the same control with `showFields={false}` to state the unlocking scope, because `requestPeriodUnlock` derives a request scope from the locked period - a selectable branch there would print a scope the approval cannot honour, so it is deliberately presented rather than faked (recorded as a known limitation needing a new temporary-unlock enforcement rule).

### Organisation and branch scope checkpoint - 2026-09-14

One reusable Organisation & Branch scope pattern now serves Period Locking, Chart of Accounts and Create Journal Entry -> Journal Lines. `src/organisation-scope.js` is the single source of truth (pure data, no React or storage): `getScopeVisibility({organisations,organisationIds|companyIds,branchIds,label})` returns the rows, the auto-resolved branch, the `strip` context line and the flags `showOrganisation` / `showBranch` (aliases `showOrgColumn` / `showBranchColumn`) from `orgCount > 1` and `orgCount > 1 || anySelectedOrgHasMultipleBranches`; `getBranchesForOrganisation(reference,organisations)`, `organisationBranches`, `selectedOrganisations`, `normaliseScope`, `validateScope` (codes `OK` / `NO_SCOPE` / `UNKNOWN_ORGANISATION` / `BRANCH_NOT_IN_ORGANISATION` / `BRANCH_REQUIRED`), `assertValidScope` and `scopeLabel` complete it. `NO_BRANCH` is an em dash and `ALL_BRANCHES` is `All branches`; a one-option branch and a branchless organisation always resolve themselves, and a branch can only ever come from the organisation that owns it.

Consumers: `src/period-locking.js` deleted its private `scopeVisibility` / `normaliseScope` copies and re-exports the shared ones (watch for orphaned fragments if that file is edited again - a leftover body once made the module a `SyntaxError: Illegal return statement`); `src/PeriodLockSettings.jsx` `ScopeSelector` reads `getScopeVisibility` and `getBranchesForOrganisation` and keeps exactly one `scopeVisibility(` call; `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` validates `createLock` through `validateScope` and shows a `.pc-lock-scope` `Locking for:` chip in the Create Lock drawer; `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx` keeps its `showOrganizationColumn` / `showBranchColumn` / `branchNamesFor` / `alignLine` contract but computes it from the shared module for names, not ids; `src/EnterpriseAccountForm.jsx` and `src/AccountWorkspace.jsx` use the same helpers for account scope, and the Chart of Accounts grid renders Organisation / Branch columns only when the shared flags say so (hidden by default, so the default render is unchanged).

Deliberate exception to record: in the Create Account drawer the Organisation multi-select is the customisation control and is never hidden, because hiding it would make adding a second organisation impossible. Only the Branch picker follows the auto-resolve rule there; the resolved scope is stated in the new `.am-scope-strip` line. ### Period Lock request register checkpoint - 2026-09-14

The Requests table in Period Lock is now one pageless register with a combined scope column and a Status column, and the register toolbar no longer prints a row count.

What changed. `REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE`, the `useState(1)` page number, the `pageCount/active/visible` slice and the `nav.pc-request-pager` block were removed from `RequestList`, so every filtered request renders in one pass; `IconChevronLeft` / `IconChevronRight` left the page import with them, and the dead `.pc-request-pager`, `.pc-pager-status` and `.pc-filter-count` families (plus the two flex overrides for the count) were deleted from `src/period-closing.css`. `span.pc-filter-count` was removed from `div.pc-register-bar`, so the toolbar is now the `Locked Periods` / `Requests` toggle, the search box and `Filters`; the toggle badges still carry each half's count.

`RequestTable({requests,periods=[],actions})` carries seven columns - Period, one combined `Organisation & Branch` cell, Reason, Requested, Requested By, Status and Actions. `requestScopeOf(request,periods)` finds the period a request belongs to and prints its `organisationName` (falling back to `demoOrganizations` by code, so a stored `ABC01` never reaches the screen - the seed periods carry only `companyId`) with `periodBranchLabel()` of that period as the small line beneath it: `All branches` for a whole-period lock, real branch names for a branch scope, and `All branches` again when no period matches. `requestStatusLabel(request)` maps the stored `Pending approval` to `Requested` and passes `Approved`, `Cancelled`, `Rejected` and any other stored status through unchanged, and the cell reuses the page's `span.pc-badge` with the `.requested` / `.approved` / `.cancelled` / `.rejected` tones added in `src/period-closing-focus.css`, which also re-specifies the seven column widths (12 / 19 / 21 / 11 / 11 / 12 / 14%) and drops the pager gutter rule. `LockRegister` passes `periods` into `RequestList`.

Also fixed: the Branch filter was rendered in the Requests half but `filterRequests()` ignored it - it now applies `branchMatch` exactly as `filterPeriods()` does.

Untouched on purpose: no engine, storage, audit, approval or lock rule changed, so `tests/period-locking.test.mjs` stays at 28/28 and the request status values stored in `wayvida-period-control-v1` keep their existing words (`Requested` is a display label only).

### Period Lock request row actions checkpoint - 2026-09-14

The Requests table's Actions cell is now one shape for every row: a read-only `View` button plus one 3-dot menu. `function RequestActions` in `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` lost its three early returns and its inline buttons, and its single return reads `<div className="pc-request-actions"><button type="button" className="pc-request-view" onClick={()=>onDetails(request)}>View</button>{note&&<span className="pc-action-note">{note}</span>}{menu}</div>`.

The menu (`details.pc-kebab.pc-request-kebab` > `div.pc-kebab-menu.pc-request-kebab-menu`) holds four entries in this order - `Approve` (`canDecide=canApprove&&decidable&&pending`), `Cancel` (`canCancel=self&&pending`, unchanged and still requester-only), `View Details` (always), `Delete` (`canDelete=self||canApprove`, `className="pc-kebab-danger"`, `IconTrash`). Both View entries call `onDetails(request)`. The `note` value keeps the two explanations that used to sit beside the buttons - `Approval requires an approver role.` and `Another approver must review your own request.` - so a missing `Approve` is still explained.

`onDeleteRequest` is threaded the same way as `onCancelRequest`: page handler `deleteRequest` -> `LockRegister` / `RequestsDrawer` -> `RequestList` -> `RequestActions`. The page calls the new engine verb `deleteUnlockRequest(state,{requestId,user,now})` in `src/period-locking.js`, which refuses an unknown id and any role that is neither the requester nor an approver, routes a `Pending approval` request through `cancelUnlockRequest` first (restoring the previous lock status and writing the `Unlock cancelled` entry) so a live request can never strand its period, then removes the record and prepends `Unlock request deleted`. Deleting a decided request never rewrites its outcome.

CSS: `src/period-closing.css` dropped the now-dead `.pc .pc-request-actions button.pc-quiet` and `button.primary` rules (`pc-quiet` is gone from `src/` entirely) and added `button.pc-request-view` (neutral secondary) and `.pc .pc-request-kebab-menu button.pc-kebab-danger` (`#b42318`, `#fff5f6` hover).

Validation: `node tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` 27/27 (two contract names changed, the Approve Unlock / pc-quiet / button.primary assertions replaced by pc-request-view, pc-kebab-danger, canDelete, IconTrash and the four-label menu), `node tests/period-locking.test.mjs` 28/28 (the new 28th contract proves the pending period is released, both audit entries survive and other roles are refused), `node tmp/pc15-requests.mjs`, `node tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` (12 surfaces), `node tmp/pc5-validate.mjs` (17 surfaces - both had to be re-pointed because `tmp/pc2-probe.jsx` was a stale snapshot), `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` 49/49, `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed, and the full sweep `FILES=64 green=50 tests=353 passed=342 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)` - the same eleven pre-existing failures. No painted browser pass and no production build (esbuild cannot spawn). The dev server at localhost:5173 serves the change live.

### Period Lock attention pill and request row checkpoint - 2026-09-14 (superseded in part by the request row actions checkpoint above)

Two changes on the same page, both presentational, both in `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` (`src/period-closing.css`, `src/period-closing-focus.css`).

`function RequestActions` no longer renders the reviewer note: the `{self&&<span className="pc-action-note">{'Recorded as '+approver+', the next authorised approver.'}</span>}` span is gone from its final `return`, so a pending request raised by the acting role now shows the emphasised `Approve Unlock`, the requester-only quiet `Cancel request` and the row's 3-dot menu as the whole Actions cell. `approver` (resolved by the page from `unlockApproverFor(requestedBy, settings)`) still drives `decidable=!self||Boolean(approver)` and the decision is still recorded under that name; only the on-screen restatement was removed. `Another approver must review your own request.` and `Approval requires an approver role.` are unchanged.

`Before you close` stopped being a one-line strip under the heading. It is now a compact, never-dismissible attention pill in the heading action group, and the names to continue with are: `details.pc-attention-pop` > `summary.pc-attention-pill` (`className={'pc-attention-pill '+attention.tone}`, tones `neutral` / `warn` / `danger`), `span.pc-attention-count` carrying `aria-live="polite"` and the count as text, `div.pc-attention-pop-menu` > `div.pc-attention-pop-head` + `<Checklist ... only/>` + `button.pc-attention-pop-all` (`Review all`). One `attention` memo (`{checks,count,tone,noun,verb,entries}`) is the single computed value behind the pill, its tone, the popover summary and the count, so they cannot disagree; `noun`/`verb` handle the singular (`1 check needs attention`) and `entries` handles `1 entry affected` versus `N entries affected`. The pill renders only while `attention.count>0`, so zero checks hides the whole `details` and `All checks passed` is printed inside the popover only. It carries no chevron and never the primary blue, and the popover deliberately holds no close action: `Create Lock / Close` stays the only place to act. `openMenus()` now includes `.pc-attention-pop[open]`, so the pill dismisses on an outside pointerdown or Escape like the other disclosures, and `.pc-focused>.pc-heading` is `position:sticky` inside the `.pc` page scroller so the count stays visible while the register scrolls; collapsing the `details` returns the row height.

`function Checklist({readiness,onNavigate,describe=true,only=false})` gained the `only` prop, which filters `Passed` out, so the same renderer draws the popover's failing-checks-only list and the pre-close drawer's full checklist.

Validation: `node tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` 27/27, `node tests/journal-actions-ui.test.mjs` 5/5 (its dismissal contract had to be widened to the four-selector `openMenus()` string), `node tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs`, `node tmp/pc15-requests.mjs`, `node tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs`, `node tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` (12 surfaces), `node tmp/pc5-validate.mjs` (17 surfaces), `node tmp/check-all-jsx.mjs` 49/49, `node scripts/validate-knowledge-docs.mjs` passed, and the full sweep `FILES=64 green=50 tests=352 passed=341 failed=11` with `CANNOT-START(4)` - the same eleven pre-existing failures. No painted browser pass and no production build (esbuild cannot spawn). The dev server at localhost:5173 serves the change live.

### Period Lock request table checkpoint - 2026-09-14 (superseded in part by the attention pill checkpoint and by the request row actions checkpoint above)

- The user decision: `in period closing page, Request section Shows only the list view/table view, need Period, Reason, Requested Requested By, Actions (Approve and Cancel) 3 dot (Approve, cancel and View Details)`. This supersedes the cards-or-list checkpoint below in full: the switch, `div.pc-request-view-bar` and every `article.pc-request-card` rule are deleted, so the `Requests` half of the register is table-only.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx`: `const [requestLayout,setRequestLayout]=useState('cards')` and the requests-branch switch markup are gone, and the requests tab renders `<RequestList ... />` with no `layout` prop. `RequestList` always renders `div.pc-request-list` around the table, keeps `REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE=6` and its own page state, and gained `const [detail,setDetail]=useState(null)` so a row menu can open one request in a portalled drawer.
- `function RequestTable({requests,actions})` renders `div.pc-table-fit pc-request-table` - the same fit shell the locked-periods table uses, so no horizontal scrolling - with five columns: Period (plus the organisation as the cell small line), Reason, Requested, Requested By, Actions. Unlock Scope and Approver left the table; their detail now lives in the request popup.
- `function RequestActions({request,canApprove,approver='',role,onDecide,onCancelRequest,onDetails})` renders the whole Actions cell. It keeps `self` / `decidable=!self||Boolean(approver)` and adds `pending=request.status==='Pending approval'`, `canDecide=canApprove&&decidable&&pending` and `canCancel=self&&pending`: a pending decidable row shows the emphasised `Approve Unlock` then the quiet requester-only `Cancel request`, a decided row shows the menu alone, and `!canApprove` / `!decidable` still print `Approval requires an approver role.` and `Another approver must review your own request.` beside the menu. No `Reject` and no status chip anywhere.
- The 3-dot menu is `<details className="pc-kebab pc-request-kebab"><summary aria-label={'Actions for the '+request.period+' unlock request'}><IconDots size={17}/></summary><div className="pc-kebab-menu pc-request-kebab-menu">` holding `Approve`, `Cancel` and `View Details` (`IconEye`). It reuses the canonical `.pc-kebab` geometry, the local `pick` helper that clears `open` before running the handler, and the page's existing outside-pointerdown / Escape dismissal, because `openMenus()` already queries `.pc-kebab[open]`. `IconEye` was added to the `@tabler/icons-react` import.
- `function requestScopeLabel(request)` is the single `Selected date range` resolver, and `function RequestDetailsDrawer({request,onClose})` renders one request as `button.pc-history-scrim` + `aside.pc-history-drawer.pc-request-drawer` (`aria-label="Unlock request details"`) whose `pc-drawer-body` holds `dl.pc-request-facts` with Requested By, Requested, Reason, Unlock scope and Approver, then Unlock duration, Access until and Approver remarks only when present.
- `src/period-closing.css`: every `.pc-request-card*` rule is deleted (including `.pc-request-card dl` inside the 1100px media query), `.pc-request-list` is now `display:block;padding:0`, `.pc-request-actions` gained `justify-content:flex-end;flex-wrap:wrap`, `.pc .pc-request-kebab{flex:none;position:relative}` and its hover were added, `.pc-request-facts` styles were added at the 12px floor, `.pc-requests-drawer` widened to `min(820px,96vw)` and `.pc-request-drawer{width:min(460px,96vw)!important}` was added. `src/period-closing-focus.css` replaced the switch and scroll block with `.pc .pc-request-list{padding:0}`, the pager padding, `th{white-space:nowrap}`, `td{vertical-align:top}`, five `th:nth-child(n)` widths (19/25/13/13/30%), the `td small` line, the actions-cell `vertical-align:middle;white-space:normal` with the wrapping note, and `@media(max-width:1100px){.pc-request-table th:nth-child(n){width:auto}}`. `pc-request-none` and the dead `pc-request-list-table` marker class are gone.

### Period Lock request decisions, pagination, lock type and More actions checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` - `RequestList` stopped rendering the per-request status badge. The card header used to print `<span className={'pc-badge '+String(request.status).toLowerCase().replaceAll(' ','-')}>{request.status}</span>`; it now holds only the period and the organisation, and a pending request is identified by its decision footer, which was already the only place it could be actioned. The card keeps its `pending` / `decided` class and nothing else about the card chrome changed.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` - the reject option is deleted and the decision actions are reordered into one group. Where the footer previously rendered `Reject` beside `Approve Unlock` plus a second, separate `<div className="pc-request-actions">` holding `Cancel request` for the requester, it now renders a single group: `{self&&<button className="pc-quiet" onClick={()=>onCancelRequest(request)}>Cancel request</button>}` then `<button className="primary" onClick={()=>onDecide(request,'Approved')}><IconCheck size={15}/>Approve Unlock</button>`. So approving is the one emphasised action and withdrawing is the quiet secondary one, and `onDecide` is only ever called with `'Approved'` from the list. `decideUnlockRequest(state, {requestId, decision, user, reason})` in `src/period-locking.js` still accepts `'Rejected'` and was not touched, and `REQUEST_STATUS_FILTERS` still lists `Rejected` so the register's Status filter keeps matching any request already stored with that status - that constant is now the only place the removed action is named. No storage key, audit entry, lock or engine rule changed.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` - the request list pages six at a time. `const REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE=6;` and `const [page,setPage]=useState(1);` live inside `RequestList`; `const pageCount=Math.ceil(requests.length/REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE),active=Math.min(Math.max(page,1),pageCount),visible=pageCount>1?requests.slice((active-1)*REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE,active*REQUEST_PAGE_SIZE):requests;` derive the slice, so a list that fits one page renders whole, and only `pageCount>1` renders `<nav className="pc-request-pager" aria-label="Unlock request pages">` with `Previous`, `Page <n> of <n>` and `Next` (the step buttons disable at each end). `RequestList` is used by both the register's `Requests` half and the `RequestsDrawer`, so both surfaces page.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` - `DetailsDrawer` no longer prints a lock type. The `<div><dt>Lock type</dt><dd>{period.lockType...}</dd></div>` row left the `pc-detail-summary` list, so the summary shows Status, the closed-on date or last action, and (when closed) the closer. The engine still validates `['Soft Lock','Hard Lock']` in `lockPeriod`, `newForm` / `newLockForm` still carry a fixed `lockType:'Hard Lock'`, and the `Closed Type` filter (`CLOSED_TYPES=['All','Manual','Automatic']`) stays, because Auto/Manual is the product's own lock vocabulary; no lock, journal, audit or storage behaviour changed.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` and `src/period-closing.css` - the `More actions` button is restyled. The summary now renders `<IconChevronDown className="pc-more-caret" size={16}/>` after the label. `src/period-closing.css` gained a transition on the base `.pc-more>summary` rule plus `.pc-more>summary:hover{border-color:#b9c9e3;background:#f8faff}`, `.pc-more>summary:focus-visible{outline:3px solid #a8c2ff;outline-offset:2px}`, `.pc-more-caret{...transition:transform .15s ease,color .15s ease}`, `.pc-more>summary:hover .pc-more-caret`, `.pc-more[open]>summary{border-color:#3478f6;background:#f2f7ff;color:#245fd9;box-shadow:0 0 0 3px #eaf1ff}` and `.pc-more[open]>summary .pc-more-caret{color:#245fd9;transform:rotate(180deg)}`. The menu rule now anchors with `top:calc(100% + 6px)` and caps at `max-height:min(70vh,420px);overflow-y:auto` instead of growing past the viewport, and `.pc-more-menu button:focus-visible` plus an icon colour transition were added to the rows. The box parity asserted by `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` is deliberately preserved: `.pc-more{position:relative;display:flex;min-width:0}` and the summary's `flex:1 1 auto`, `min-height:40px`, `border-radius:7px`, `padding:0 15px`, `gap:7px`, `font-size:14px`, `font-weight:600` and `white-space:nowrap` still match `.pc-focused>.pc-heading-actions button`.
- `src/PeriodClosing.jsx` - every disclosure menu on the page now dismisses itself. A native details element closes only when its own `summary` is clicked, so `PeriodClosing` gained one `useEffect` that registers a `pointerdown` listener (removing `open` from each of `.pc-more`, `.pc-kebab` and `.pc-filters-more` whose subtree does not contain the event target) and a `keydown` listener (removing `open` from all three on `Escape`), and removes both on unmount. `useEffect` was added to the existing `react` import. This changes three existing menus, not only `More actions`.
- `src/period-closing.css` - the decision and pager styles were added under the request-card block: `.pc .pc-request-actions{align-items:center;gap:8px}`, `.pc .pc-request-actions button.pc-quiet{border-color:#e1e6ed;background:#fff;color:#475467;font-weight:600}`, `.pc .pc-request-actions button.pc-quiet:hover{border-color:#e4bcc2;background:#fff5f6;color:#b42318}`, `.pc .pc-request-actions button.primary{min-height:36px;padding:6px 14px;font-weight:650;box-shadow:0 2px 8px #326df433}`, `.pc-request-pager{display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:flex-end;gap:10px;padding:2px 2px 0}`, `.pc-request-pager button{min-height:34px;padding:5px 12px;gap:5px;font-size:12px;font-weight:600}` and `.pc-pager-status{color:#667085;font-size:12px;font-weight:600;white-space:nowrap}`. Every new label is 12px, so the AGENTS.md minimum text size holds. The `.pc-request-card>footer` wrap rules from the previous checkpoint are unchanged.

### Period Lock style and drawer geometry checkpoint - 2026-09-14

The reported popup, overlap and padding breakage in Period Lock had one root cause: every drawer is rendered with `createPortal(..., document.body)`, so the `.pc aside` reset in `src/period-closing-focus.css` never matched and the legacy shell rule `aside{position:fixed;z-index:20;inset:0 auto 0 0;width:206px}` gave each drawer the 206px navigation rail geometry on top of the sidebar. The history and unlock-request portals are now wrapped in `<div className="pc pc-portal-scope">` (`.pc.pc-portal-scope{display:contents}`) and `src/period-closing.css` carries the canonical shell: a scrim at `z-index:1390`, the drawer at `z-index:1400` with `position:fixed!important` and `right:0!important`, `width:min(<n>px,96vw)!important`, a fixed 18px/22px header, a footer that stays put, and `pc-drawer-body` as the only scroll container.
The Period Locking Settings drawer is the deliberate exception to the wrapper rule: it keeps the canonical `.pc-history-drawer` shell class but not the `.pc` scope wrapper, so the page base-control rules (`.pc button`, `.pc input`, `.pc select`) cannot reach the panel. `src/period-lock-settings.css` therefore neutralises the legacy global top-bar `header` rule for its own headings with `.pls header{position:static;inset:auto;width:auto;height:auto;min-height:0;max-width:none;z-index:auto;margin:0}`. Without that reset every `.pls-card>header` inherited `position:fixed;height:58px` and the `.pls-card>header` titles stacked on top of each other at the top of the viewport. Widths are 1080px for the wide lock history register (`min(820px,94vw)` between 760px and 1100px), 620px for settings, 560px for lock, override, audit and details, and 520px for unlock requests. The register nested boxes were flattened to one gutter per level, `.pc-heading` now uses the Journal Entries heading grid, the status toggle is wrapped in a labelled `pc-filter`, the `Before you close`, `Activity History` and settings card headers reset the legacy top-bar `header` styling, and the sub-900px current-period layout uses explicit grid placement instead of `margin-left:54px`. `src/PeriodLockSettings.jsx` needed no markup change for that geometry fix: `.pls-popup` stays `position:static` inside the drawer host with a sticky heading and footer. (Its markup did change later the same day for content reasons - see the settings popup consolidation checkpoint above.) No storage key, journal, close, lock, audit or enforcement rule changed. `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` reports **16 passed, 0 failed** including two new geometry contracts, with `period-lock-settings-ui` 13/13, `period-locking` 24/24, `period-scope-visibility` 16/16, `period-unlock-scope` 9/9 and `journal-create-ui` 22/22; the full suite is 306 passed / 11 pre-existing failures / 4 esbuild-blocked, and a tag-stack probe over the 13 SSR surfaces reports no nested or overlapping elements. No rendered visual pass or production build was possible: in-app browser automation is denied by the environment auto-review and `vite build` needs the esbuild binary the sandbox cannot spawn.

### Period Lock register views and single locking switch checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The register panel header is now the `Locked Periods` / `Requests` toggle alone, on the left. The `Locked periods` / `Unlock requests` heading and the `Every lock for <organisation> in the selected financial year.` / `Every reopen request for <organisation>, newest first.` notes are gone from the main register and survive only in the lock history drawer, which passes `title`/`note` and no `onView`. `LockRegister` gained `mode` (default `'Off'`), `title`/`note` default to `''`, and the panel header only renders when it has content.
- New engine export `lockRegisterRows(periods,{mode='Manual',today='',createdIds=[]}={})` decides what the lock list may show and is consumed once by the `historyRows` memo: `Automatic` keeps only locks whose period already ended (`period.end<today` and a `Closed` display status), `Manual` keeps only periods listed in `createdIds`, `Off` hides nothing. `createdLockIds` comes from the creation audit entries (`periodId` of `Lock period created` / `Period closed`), so seeded demonstration locks stay out of the manual register, a created lock appears immediately and survives a reload, and the history drawer (`allLockRows`) is unaffected. The manual empty state is `No locks created yet. Use Create Lock / Close to lock a period for every module.`; the register `Period` select now offers only the periods its rows can show plus the current selection. `manual` and `lockMode` moved above the register memos so the memo can read them.
- Period Lock Settings leads with one sentence (`Locking applies to every module in Wayvida Books, and changes apply from the next unclosed period.`). The `Unlock approval` card is the `Require approval to unlock` switch alone - the role select is gone - and the `Unlock defaults` card (default/maximum unlock duration, unlock scope, automatic re-lock, daily auto-approval) was deleted from `More policy settings`; the engine keeps those values as `normalisePeriodSettings` defaults and `settingsWarnings()` still offers `Enable auto-approval for daily locks`. The locking-off note now says existing locks keep protecting their period until an administrator unlocks them.
- The `Period locking` switch is now genuinely product-wide. `src/AccountingSettingsPanel.jsx` (`Period control`) gained a `Period locking` switch that writes `lockingMode` (`Off` <-> `Manual`), rebound `Automatic period closing` from the legacy `autoLockPreviousMonth` flag to `lockingMode` (`Automatic` <-> `Manual`), disabled the automatic switch while locking is off, and pointed `Closing frequency` / `Close after days` at `schedule.frequency` / `schedule.lockAfterDays` (the values the scheduler and the popup read) instead of the unused `defaultFrequency` / `autoLockAfterDays`. Both surfaces write through `normalisePeriodSettings` and `writePeriodSettings`. This repaired a defect: the old Settings toggle wrote the derived mirror, which `normalisePeriodSettings` overwrote on save, so it never changed the locking mode.
- Validation: `tests/period-closing-ui.test.mjs` 17/17, `tests/period-lock-settings-ui.test.mjs` 14/14, `tests/period-locking.test.mjs` 26/26, `period-scope-visibility` 16/16, `period-unlock-scope` 9/9, `ui-quality` 5/5; `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` (9184 chars), `tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` (12 surfaces) and `tmp/pc5-validate.mjs` (15 surfaces) pass; the dev server at `http://localhost:5173/` serves the new markup. ### Period Lock header consolidation checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- Period Lock now has one compact heading row: the `Period Lock` title paired with the read-only locking policy tag (`pc-title-block` + `pc-policy-chip`). The page subtitle, the composite period/status/scope line (`pc-composite-line`), the locks status line (`pc-status-line`, `pc-status-link`) and the inline missing-periods row (`pc-missing`) were deleted.
- One right-aligned `More actions` disclosure (`details.pc-more` > `summary.pc-more-btn` + `.pc-more-menu`) replaces the chip / Settings / History / primary cluster. Entries in order: Period closing settings, Before you close (badge counting the checks that need attention, plus the entries-affected count), Audit log / History (last-locked period with actor and date in the tooltip, locked count and reopen requests in the line), Create Lock / Close (only while locking is on and the role may lock), Unlock requests (only while approval is on, with the pending count) and Create missing periods (only while periods are missing and the role may configure). The summary carries a red `pc-more-count` whenever anything inside needs attention and every entry closes the menu before acting.
- The `Before you close` checklist moved out of the page body into a `pc-before-drawer` right-side drawer opened from `More actions`; it keeps the entries-affected count, the `pc-impact-chips` category row, the readiness pill and the per-check links. The `Transactions` / `Audit log` / `Close with review` link row is gone and `automaticClose` was removed, so `Create Lock / Close` is the single close entry point.
- `Create Lock / Close` is mode aware. Manual locking keeps `Select period` / `Date` / `Reason (optional)` and locks every module. Automatic locking renders `Frequency`, `Effective from`, `Lock after period end`, `Lock at` and `Notify before locking` from `settings.schedule` (`LOCK_FREQUENCIES`, `SCHEDULE_CHOICES`), resolves the next unlocked period through the new `autoTarget()` helper (walks `generatedSchedule()`, skips already-locked or conflicted ranges) and prints it as `Next lock: <period> · <start> to <end> · ...`; when nothing is free the preview turns amber and the submit is disabled. Lock creation still runs through `createLockWindow()` + `lockPeriod()`, so no close, lock, audit or storage rule changed.
- Dead CSS (`.pc-composite-line`, `.pc-status-line`, `.pc-status-link`, `.pc-link-action`, `.pc-current-actions`, `.pc-missing`) was removed alongside the markup, and `.pc-more*`, `.pc-lock-fields`, `.pc-lock-preview[.blocked]`, `.pc-before-drawer` and the one-row `.pc-title-block` were added. No removed class name survives anywhere in `src/`.

SSR harnesses `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` (313957 chars), `tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` (12 surfaces) and `tmp/pc5-validate.mjs` (15 surfaces well formed) pass, all 48 `src/*.jsx` transform, and the dev server at `http://localhost:5173/` serves the new markup and CSS. ### Period locking enforcement checkpoint - 2026-09-14

- The unlock lifecycle is now complete in `src/period-locking.js`. `unlockWindowFor()` resolves a requested scope against the lock: `Whole period` records the full range, and `Selected date range` requires both dates, requires start on or before end, and throws when the range is not completely inside the locked period. `requestPeriodUnlock()` stores `unlockScope` / `unlockStartDate` / `unlockEndDate` on the request and its `Unlock requested` audit entry, and `decideUnlockRequest()` copies the window onto the period.
- `validatePeriodAction()` allows only dates inside an approved window, so a date in the same period but outside the window stays blocked with `PERIOD_HARD_LOCKED` and a message naming the window; `blockingLocksFor()` applies the same window so overlapping locks stay independently enforceable. `LOCK_ACTIVE_STATUSES` now includes `Temporary Unlock`.
- New `cancelUnlockRequest(state, {requestId, user, reason, now})` is limited to the requester or an approver while the request is `Pending approval`; it restores the previous lock status, marks the request `Cancelled` with `cancelledBy`/`cancelledAt`/`decisionReason` and writes an `Unlock cancelled` audit entry. `relockExpiredPeriods()` now stamps the request `Expired` with `expiredAt` while keeping its history, clears the window fields, and writes two `System`-attributed audit entries, `Unlock expired` then `Period reclosed`, both carrying the request id. New `recordPolicyChange()` writes nothing when the policy is unchanged and otherwise audits a `Policy changed` event with `beforeValue`, `afterValue` and the `policyChangeGuard()` message.
- Enforcement now fails closed when the resolved lock state cannot be read: `assertOperationAllowed()` returns `allowed:false` with code `PERIOD_NOT_CONFIGURED` and an explanatory remedy instead of throwing inside a posting path.
- Every locking audit entry is written through one shared record builder, so each event carries `user`, `date`, `action`, `organisation`, `branch`, `beforeValue`, `afterValue`, `reason`, `periodId` and `requestId`. Organisation names resolve from the period, the request or the shared code map; a global policy change is attributed to `All organisations`.
- Period Lock exposes the lifecycle: the unlock drawer collects the requested scope with bounded start and end dates, the requests panel shows each request's scope, and a pending request raised by the current simulated role shows a `Cancel request` action.
- Focused tests passed: **9 passed, 0 failed** (`node tests/period-unlock-scope.test.mjs`, new) covering window containment, escaping ranges, whole-period and date-range requests, an approved window releasing only its own dates, the overlap rule, cancellation, expiry with both audit events, the audited policy change, the fail-closed `ENFORCED_OPERATIONS` sweep and the required audit record fields.
- Regression suites re-run in the same session: `period-locking` 24/24, `period-scope-visibility` 16/16 (one stale expiry assertion was updated to assert both audit events, matching its own test name and the specification), `period-lock-settings-ui` 13/13, `period-closing-ui` 11/11 and `journal-create-ui` 22/22. The sandbox SSR harnesses `tmp/pc2-ssr.mjs` (7382 chars) and `tmp/pc2-drawers.mjs` (12 surfaces) pass, and the extended harness also asserts the new unlock scope control, the hidden date fields for a whole-period request and the bounded range inputs. All 48 `src/*.jsx` files still transform through the sandbox JSX pipeline.
- Full-suite sweep in the same session: 61 test files, 51 green. The 10 failing files are exactly the previously documented unrelated failures (`account-enterprise`, `account-master`, `create-page-navigation-ui`, `item-tax-ui`, `journal-business-view`, `journal-detail-ui`, `journal-heading-count-ui`, `operational-modules-ui`, `settings-ui`, `wayvida-date-picker-ui`) and were left untouched.
- Not verified: no rendered browser or human visual pass (in-app browser and localhost tooling stay blocked by the environment auto-review) and no production build, because `vite build` needs the esbuild binary this sandbox cannot spawn (`spawn EPERM`). The unlock scope inputs are the first fields in this module that need a real visual check.

### Previously completed: Period Lock close-books workspace

Period Lock was rebuilt on 2026-09-12 into a task-oriented close-books workspace in `src/PeriodClosing.jsx`, styled by `src/period-closing.css` and powered additively by `src/period-locking.js`. The screen was later restructured (see the 2026-09-14 checkpoint above) from four summary cards above four tabs into one composite title line, one status line, three tabs and a Settings button.

Closing is validation-gated. `evaluateClosingReadiness()` runs five professional checks — invoices posted, bills approved, no draft journal entries, bank reconciliation completed, tax adjustments completed. Unposted invoices, unapproved purchase bills and draft journals block the close; reconciliation and tax warnings must be acknowledged in the confirmation drawer before `closePeriod()` performs the OPEN to CLOSED transition. Style: the closing reason remains mandatory, and the period stores `closedBy`, `closedAt` and a `closingChecklist` snapshot alongside a `Period closed` audit event.

Period History defaults to the current financial year instead of every stored period, and adds financial-year, organisation, branch and status filters plus search. Columns are Period, Organisation, Branch, Status, Closed Date, Closed By and Actions; the row menu exposes View Audit Log and Request Unlock (plus Close Period while the period is open). Unlock requests are raised under one simulated role and decided under another, self-approval is refused by the engine, and an approved request reopens the period with an expiry that is automatically reclosed.

The interface only ever shows four accountant-facing statuses: `Open`, `Closing`, `Closed` and `Reopened`, mapped by `closingStatus()`. The engine vocabulary and `validatePostingDate()` are unchanged, and no new storage key was added. Period policy (automatic closing, closing frequency, unlock approval requirement, allowed roles, default unlock duration) is edited from Period Lock → Settings and from Settings → Accounting → Period control in `src/AccountingSettingsPanel.jsx`; both write the single `wayvida-period-settings-v1` key.

### Previously completed: shared Wayvida date picker

Wayvida now has a shared browser-date-input presentation delegate in `src/WayvidaDatePicker.jsx`, mounted once from `src/main.jsx`. Pointer selection on any existing `input[type=date]` opens the consistent Wayvida calendar: month navigation, selected-day state, and Cancel/OK actions follow the supplied reference. Confirming sends normal native `input` and `change` events with the existing ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` value, so no module state model, min/max constraint, validation, accounting period check, or posting rule changes. Native month and time controls intentionally remain native; keyboard focus may still use the native browser picker.

The picker deliberately uses isolated `wvdp-*` selectors rather than generic `header`/`footer` tags so application shell styles cannot escape into the popup. Its position uses viewport coordinates inside a fixed overlay. The Journal Entries date filters add dedicated indicator and focus styling in `src/journal-date-filter.css`.

Journal Entries also imports `src/journal-heading-fix.css`. It uses a stable responsive two-column heading/action layout and deliberately hides the redundant create-button helper message so long page titles are not clipped.

The active Journal Entries toolbar is intentionally compact: search and status remain visible, while `src/journal-filter-menu.css` provides the **Filters** menu for entry purpose/journal type, created by, from date, and to date. Do not put those date fields back into the default grid toolbar.

Journal Entries now intentionally lists **manual journals only**. Automatic accounting entries remain available through the General Ledger, account-ledger drill-down, and reports. The same global `View As` preference changes the manual-journal UI only: Business Owner users see entry-purpose labels such as **Fix a mistake** and **Move money between accounts**, while Accountant users see professional types such as **Correction Entry** and **Transfer Journal**. Creation does not offer a Reversal Entry type; reversing a posted journal is the controlled corrective action. The register has purpose, status, and date filters; creation records submission, approval, and posting timestamps without changing the underlying lifecycle or debit/credit rules. Key source: `src/JournalEntriesPro.jsx`.

The feature is presentation and metadata-only. Journal posting, reversal, account/ledger generation, amounts, and existing stored records remain compatible. Legacy `Adjustment` and `Correction` records are displayed through the new labels.

## 3. Non-negotiable accounting safeguards

1. Never create or persist an unbalanced journal.
2. Normal operational workflows must derive accounts automatically; do not expose debit/credit selection to ordinary users.
3. Sales and purchase orders do not post accounting. Their converted documents remain drafts until explicitly posted.
4. Posted history is corrected through reversal, credit/debit note, or adjustment—not deletion or silent mutation.
5. Preserve account codes and journal references. Do not convert demonstration balances into opening journals.
6. Official reports must use posted accounting facts and distinguish unposted documents.
7. Company, branch, period, tax, customer, vendor, item, and account mappings must remain traceable.
8. Browser role selectors and UI gates are demonstrations, not production authorization.

## 4. Safe continuation workflow

1. Read `AGENTS.md` first.
2. Read `docs/README.md`, `docs/WAYVIDA-BOOKS-HANDBOOK.md`, `docs/project-manifest.json`, and the module-specific document relevant to the task.
3. Inspect the source component, domain service, persistence key, and related tests before editing.
4. Treat source and tests as authoritative if documentation differs.
5. Preserve the current Wayvida design language, navigation hierarchy, React architecture, accounting contracts, and backward-compatible stored data.
6. Make service/domain changes outside JSX when a request affects business rules.
7. Add or update tests in proportion to the risk.
8. Update this knowledge package under the mandatory documentation checkpoint in `AGENTS.md`.

### Required after every material change

The receiving AI must keep this package synchronized during development. For every material change, record:

- the implemented behavior and the reason for it;
- affected components, services, routes, storage keys, data contracts, and tests;
- accounting and backward-compatibility impact;
- exact checks that passed or failed;
- unfinished work, known limitations, and the safest next action.

Update `project-manifest.json` for machine-readable changes, the relevant modular guide for technical detail, `WAYVIDA-BOOKS-HANDBOOK.md` for essential cross-project knowledge, `TESTING-AND-QA.md` for actual verification, and this transfer guide when the continuation checkpoint changes.

Do not preserve the project by copying raw conversations. Convert chat decisions into concise, durable requirements. Exclude secrets, credentials, personal data, browser profiles, and unrelated local-machine information.

## 5. Commands

From the repository root:

```text
npm install
npm run dev
node --test tests/*.test.mjs
npm run docs:check
npm run build
npm run test:sites
```

The application runs at `http://localhost:4001/` during local development and preview. Docker/nginx static hosting uses port 4002 unless `WAYVIDA_PORT` overrides it.

Sandbox note (2026-09-14, superseded 2026-09-15): a restricted Windows workspace once used local `tmp/` esbuild shims to start Vite when the sandbox denied child processes. Those `tmp/` files were scratch workarounds, not product code, and they are no longer in the repository. Use `npm install` and `npm run dev` in a normal environment.

## 6. What must not be transferred

- Do not send `.env` files, access tokens, API keys, browser profiles, credentials, or unrelated personal files.
- Do not treat screenshots or previous chat messages as more authoritative than current source and tests.
- `node_modules/` and `dist/` are reproducible and may be omitted to reduce transfer size. Keep lockfiles.
- Browser-local business records are not contained in the Git repository. Export them separately only if the user explicitly needs the same demo/user data, and review the export for sensitive information first.

## 7. Recommended transfer contents

For a complete engineering transfer, include:

```text
AGENTS.md
docs/
src/
tests/
public/
scripts/
worker/
.openai/
package.json
package-lock.json
vite.config.mjs
index.html
Dockerfile
docker-compose.yml
docker/
.dockerignore
root feature specifications (*.md)
```

## 8. Ready-to-paste prompt

Use the maintained prompt in [AI-HANDOFF-PROMPT.md](AI-HANDOFF-PROMPT.md). Attach the repository or the transfer contents above. Tell the receiving AI the next concrete task; do not paste the entire historical chat.

## 9. Known limitations

- This remains a frontend/browser prototype, not a production multi-tenant accounting service.
- Browser storage is not transactional, tamper-proof, backed up, or shared between devices/accounts.
- UI permission labels do not provide authenticated authorization.
- Statutory, security, concurrency, disaster-recovery, and database guarantees remain future backend work.
- The production bundle currently exceeds Vite's advisory size threshold and should eventually be split by route/module.
- The legacy stylesheet `src/styles.css` still carries app-bar element rules (`header{position:fixed;height:58px}`, `header label{...}`, `header>button{...}`, and `@media(max-width:1050px){header>button:not(.primary):not(.hamb){display:none}}`) that leak into every `<header>` in the app. Components that portal a drawer or modal header must reset them, and a plain control row is safer as a `div`. These rules are the root cause of the hidden close buttons and the forced 38px filter labels; a future cleanup should scope them to the application top bar.

## Balance Sheet presentation checkpoint - 2026-09-26
